Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide xix
About This Guide
Description
The Siemens Opcenter Execution Core System Administration Guide provides information on using 
Opcenter Execution Core (Opcenter EX CR) to:
• Set up and maintain your sites 
• Define your database configuration information 
• Create and configure the metadata databases
• Configure e-mail notification
• Enable and use transaction tracing to develop, debug, and troubleshoot Configurable Logic Flows 
(CLFs)
• Configure the system for network load balancing
The guide also includes appendixes that cover:
• Database identifiers — assignment considerations and limitations
• A compilation of best practices, troubleshooting information, and related FAQs
• Transaction and security permissions associated with each Opcenter EX CR page  
• Copying the transaction database and DataStore database from one Opcenter EX CR environment 
to another
• Barcode scanning for container, carriers, and batches
• ASCII character codes
Audience
This document is intended for system administrators, network managers, and database administrator 
managers with extensive experience in Windows administration who are responsible for implementing 
and maintaining  Opcenter EX CR.
Conventions Used
For Code Samples
Code samples are provided according to the development platform supported by the product. This guide 
uses the following font convention to depict programming code:
 
' Delete the service
Set Assoc = Nothing

=== PDF PAGE 20 ===
AboutThisGuide
xx SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
ServLink.RemoveService("AService")
Important:Siemens provides sample code as is without warranty of any kind, either express or 
implied, including but not limited to the implied warranties of merchantability and/or 
fitness for a particular purpose.
Because of the limitation of the physical page, a line of code meant to occupy one line in the program 
wraps to the next line in the documentation. If this occurs, the wrapped lines are indented. For example:
 
Set Assoc = Nothing ServLink.RemoveService("AService");
 Set Assoc = New ServLink.AddService
For Specific Information Types
This guide uses these formatting conventions.
Convention Definition Example
Blue underlined text Indicates a hyperlink, and applies only 
to URLs and e-mail addresses
www.siemens.com
Bold font Indicates icon names, buttons, field 
names, and specific user entries
Click Associate on the toolbar.
Enter a value in the Item Caption field. 
Italic font Indicates a document title; also 
indicates the result of a step in a 
procedure
Refer to the Opcenter Execution Core 
Modeling User Guide.
Click Workflow. The Workflow page 
appears within the Modeling page.
Important: Indicates that the omission or result of a certain action can cause minor errors, 
but does not necessarily cause data loss and might require repeating a 
procedure or changing settings
Note: Indicates relevant information that reinforces an idea and is not critical to any 
action that you must perform
Documentation Release Number
The plus sign (+) in the documentation release number indicates the document is compatible with 
software Release 2510 and later releases. If the content of the document is updated for a subsequent main 
release such as Release 2604 or 2610, the documentation release number will be updated.

=== PDF PAGE 21 ===
AboutThisGuide
How This Guide Is Organized
This guide is organized as follows.
Chapter / Appendix Contents
Chapter 1, Management Studio Explains how to configure the Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter 
EX CR applications after installation and how to maintain 
configuration information for your sites. 
Chapter 2, Metadata Databases Discusses the purpose of the metadata databases, provides 
some sample images from both databases, describes the field 
in IMS that are related to information in the databases, and 
provides a table listing the default values in each database. 
Chapter 3, Configuring WIP E-Mail Explains how to configure Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX 
CR to send maintenance and WIP message e-mails when an 
operator completes a transaction on a resource.
Chapter 4, Tracing Transactions Explains how to enable the Transaction Tracing utility and how 
to read trace files in order to facilitate development, 
debugging, and troubleshooting of Configurable Logic Flows 
(CLFs).
Chapter 5, Clustering Application 
Servers
Explains how to cluster multiple Application Servers to balance 
the load of transactions using network load balancing.
Chapter 6, Application Server Provides information on configuring the maximum memory 
limit for the application pool, monitoring Application Server 
performance, and viewing Application Server events for 
recycling.
Appendix A, Database Identifiers Provides an overview of database identifiers, and discusses 
DBID assignment considerations and limitations. 
Appendix B, Best Practices Provides a compilation of known issues a customer may 
encounter when installing, running, and maintaining Opcenter 
EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR. A resolution is provided for each 
issue.
Appendix C, Permissions Identifies the transactions and the security permissions that are 
associated with each  page. It also identifies Portal permissions 
associated with each page and pageflow.
Appendix D, Copying a Database 
from One Environment to Another
Explains how to copy one Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR 
environment to another environment; for example, when you 
want to copy the production environment to the development 
environment for test purposes.
Appendix E, Smart Scanning Explains smart scanning concepts and explains how to 
customize and use smart scanning.
Appendix F, Setting Up the SMTP 
Server
Explains how to add a Simple Mail Transfer Protocol (SMTP) 
server after installing the application.
Appendix G, ASCII Character Codes Provides a table of support ALT-ASCII values.

AboutThisGuide
Security Information
Siemens provides products and solutions with industrial security functions that support the secure 
operation of plants, systems, machines and networks.
In order to protect plants, systems, machines and networks against cyber threats, it is necessary to 
implement – and continuously maintain – a holistic, state-of-the-art industrial security concept. Siemens’ 
products and solutions constitute one element of such a concept.
Customers are responsible for preventing unauthorized access to their plants, systems, machines and 
networks. Such systems, machines and components should only be connected to an enterprise network or 
the internet if and to the extent such a connection is necessary and only when appropriate security 
measures (e.g. firewalls and/or network segmentation) are in place.
For additional information on industrial security measures that may be implemented, please visit 
https://www.siemens.com/industrialsecurity.
Siemens’ products and solutions undergo continuous development to make them more secure. Siemens 
strongly recommends that product updates are applied as soon as they are available and that the latest 
product versions are used. Use of product versions that are no longer supported, and failure to apply the 
latest updates may increase customer’s exposure to cyber threats.
To stay informed about product updates, subscribe to the Siemens Industrial Security RSS Feed under 
https://www.siemens.com/industrialsecurity.

Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide 1-1
Chapter 1: Management Studio
 
Introduction
Management Studio enables you to configure Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR products after 
installation. These include  Manufacturing and Opcenter EX CR Intelligence. Management Studio 
consolidates basic configuration and administration tasks into a single application that is installed with the  
Administration Tools.
You use Management Studio to maintain a list of sites and configuration information for each site. A site is 
one or more Application Servers that share a transaction database. A site can be a cluster of Application 
Servers or a single Application Server.
In This Chapter
This chapter contains these topics:
• Management Studio Interface
• Managing Sites
• Opening and Saving a Configuration File
• Managing Servers
• Managing Databases
• Managing Metadata Database Configuration
• Managing Transaction Database Configuration
• Managing DataStore Database Configuration

• Procedures Required After Changing a Database User’s Password 
• Enabling Server Trace Logs
• Adding Users to Designer

• Viewing License Information

• Managing Workspaces

Configuration Procedures Overview
This list identifies the procedures required to configure Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR. These 
procedures are performed in the order listed.
1. Open Management Studio.
2. Define the database configuration for the transaction database, and optionally for the DataStore 
database.
3. Create the new site.
4. Configure the servers.
5. Configure the databases.
6. Create the database tables.
7. Configure Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR, including security and notification settings.
Detailed instructions for each procedure follow.
Event Log
All Management Studio activity is logged to the  event log.

Management Studio Interface
The Management Studio client gives you the ability to maintain a list of sites and configuration 
information for each site. The Management Studio interface displays a tree view of the configuration. The 
right pane displays information specific to your selection in the left pane.
Management Studio Window
This image shows an example of the Management Studio window.
Management Studio Warning Messages
Warning message pop-ups appear when a change you make requires additional actions before the change 
can take effect. For example, a Warning message pop-up can tell you that you must perform a database 
update, restart a server, or regenerate WCF services. Clicking the OK button closes the Warning message 
pop-up.

Configuration File Name and Location
The configuration information you enter or change is saved to a configuration file. The default 
configuration file name is Config.ims. It is stored in the InSite Administration directory. 
You can save your changes to this file or to a different file name or location. The Open Configuration 
option on the File menu enables you to open any Management Studio configuration file.
Note: The uninstall process backs up the Management Studio configuration file only if the default file 
name and file location are used.

Managing Sites
Site-specific information is critical for the designation of unique identifiers and database updates. It is not 
expected to change often.
The site name is a unique but meaningful name assigned to the Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR 
installation. For example, you could assign a site name to match a geographical location.
When Managing Sites
Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR  configuration begins with the creation of a new site. A  new site tree 
appears when you select Add Site from the Sites menu or shortcut menu.
A Sites menu appears on the Management Studio window when the Sites folder or a site tree are selected. 
Refer to "Sites Menu" for information on the Sites menu.
This image shows an example of a new site tree.
You can rename and delete sites. Additionally, you can export a site’s configuration file to another location 
for testing and backup purposes. You can also import a site into Management Studio from a previously 
exported  configuration file that contains all the configuration information for the site.
How to Add a Site
Follow these steps to add a site:
1. Click Start > Opcenter Execution MDD > Management Studio. The Management Studio 
window appears.
Or
Click Start >  Opcenter Execution Core > Management Studio. The Management Studio win-
dow appears. 
2. Right-click the Sites folder and select Add Site. A new site tree appears and the default site 
name is in edit mode.

3. Enter a  name for your site in the highlighted NewSite node.
Important:The Site Name is equal to the Site ID in previous versions of the software ; 
therefore, if you are upgrading from a previous version, you must enter the 
Site ID.
4. Press Enter. A confirmation message appears.
5. Click Yes to save the new site name to the configuration file for this site, or click No to cancel the 
site addition.
How to Rename a Site
Follow these steps to rename a site:
1. Click Start > Opcenter Execution MDD > Management Studio. The Management Studio

Chapter1:ManagementStudio
Managing Servers
You can add one or more servers to a site.
The Application Server is the core transaction server that runs the business logic. All transactions are 
processed through the Application Server, which provides these services:
• Contains the Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR product and factory information model that 
encapsulate the business rules and logic.
• Enables users to define their business rules through configurable data objects (CDOs) and 
configurable logic flows (CLFs).
•  Communicates with client applications using XML documents. It listens for incoming XML 
documents from HTTP/S client requests, parses the documents, and processes them as Opcenter 
EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR transactions. It returns an XML document of the results.
• Stores and retrieves information.
• Provides translation between the Object Model and industry-standard relational databases to 
store and retrieve data.
Note: Servers must use the same transaction database in Management Studio.  For example, your loc-
alhost and an additional remote server must both use the same transaction database.
Important:The Application Server, transaction (OLTP) database, and DataStore (ODS) database 
servers must be co-located in the same geographic  datacenter.  These servers must be 
configured for the same time zone, and be physically on the same VLAN (one hop).  
Ideally, all of these servers are connected to the same network switch. The network 
latency from the Application Server to the OLTP database server should optimally be less 
than 1 millisecond, but no more than 5 milliseconds to ensure the best performance of 
Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR products.
When Adding Host Information for a Server
Host information for a server appears in the right pane of the Management Studio window when you 
select an existing server or add a new server.
A Camstar Servers menu appears on the Management Studio window when the Camstar Servers folder or 
server nodes are selected. Refer to "Camstar Servers Menu" for information on the Camstar Servers menu.
The Save and Discard buttons are disabled until you enter text in the Hostname or IP Address field. Click 
Save to save your changes. Click Discard to undo your changes and restore the previous field values.
A message prompts you to save or cancel if you try to close the Host Information pane without discarding 
or saving your changes. Follow the directions to save or discard your changes.

Managing Databases
The next step in the configuration process is to create the databases. There are three types of databases 
you can add.
Type Definition
Metadata The databases, InSite.mdb and SiteInfo.mdb, are used by Designer. These databases 
contain Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR configuration information.
Transaction The transaction database is the main production database used by Opcenter EX 
MDD or Opcenter EX CR. It stores all your Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR 
information such as tables and instances. Also called OLTP.
DataStore DataStore is an operational database that is separate from the transaction 
(production) database. It is only required if you will be running DataStore.
Metadata fields, tables, relationships, instance data, indexes, stored procedures, and primary keys in the 
production database are:
• Created through the Create New Database Tables menu option, and 
• Updated by the Update Database menu option.
Refer to the current Release Matrix for OC EX CR on the Support Center for information on supported 
database versions.
When Creating Databases
You must create a:
• Metadata database before you can create a Transaction database. 
• Transaction database before you can create a DataStore database.
These two databases are required. The DataStore database is only required if you have DataStore installed.
The metadata database uses the Designer data sources, InSite.mdb and SiteInfo.mdb, which are created by 
the install. The Designer and InSite SiteInfo are automatically defined with the Administration and 
Configuration component during installation.
Note: In a clustered environment, verify that each member of the cluster has the same settings for 
the ODBC info and site info tabs.
When Updating Databases
The Update Databases selection is capable of sending updates to your different database types, such as 
transaction or operational databases, as long as the database configurations  are in place.
Special Note on Oracle Databases
Oracle Enterprise Edition does not lock database tables while the indices are rebuilt. Therefore, all 
Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR transactions will run successfully during this update process.

=== PDF PAGE 43 ===
Chapter1:ManagementStudio
However, if you are using Oracle Standard Edition, database tables may be temporarily locked while the 
indices are rebuilt. Some Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR transactions may fail if the transaction is 
trying to access a temporarily locked table. Therefore, it is recommended that you do not run Opcenter EX 
MDD or Opcenter EX CR transactions during a database update with Oracle Standard Edition.
When Deleting Databases
Databases must be deleted in reverse order of how they were created. For example, you must delete the 
DataStore database before you can delete the Transaction database. You must also delete the Transaction 
database before you can delete the Metadata database.
Deleting a database in Management Studio does not affect the data in the database. It only removes the 
entry in the .mdb.
Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide 1-19

How to Add and Configure a Metadata Database
Follow these steps to add and configure a Metadata database:
1. Click Start > Opcenter Execution MDD > Management Studio. The Management Studio 
window appears.
Or
Click Start >  Opcenter Execution Core > Management Studio. The Management Studio win-
dow appears.
2. Right-click the Databases folder and select Add Metadata Database. A Metadata Database icon 
appears and the Metadata Database Configuration information appears in the right pane.
3. Click Browse for the Designer Metadata Database field to open the Select a metadata database 
window. The InSite.mdb is the default selection. 
4. Click Open to select the default database or select a different database if necessary. The database 
selection appears in the Designer Metadata Database field.
5. Click Browse for the Site Info Database Filename field to open the Select a site info database 
window. The SiteInfo.mdb is the default selection. 
6. Click Open to select the default database or select a different database if necessary. The database 
selection appears in the Site Info Database Filename field.
7. Click Test Connect for each database. Management Studio displays a “Connection Established” 
message if the connection is successfully established. Otherwise, the application displays an 
error message.
8. Click OK to close the message.
9. Click Save. Management Studio displays a success message.

Managing Transaction Database Configuration
The Transaction database is required. You can create the Transaction database after creating the metadata 
database. 
Transaction Database Configuration Information
Configuration information for the Transaction database appears in the right pane of the Management 
Studio window when you  add or select the Transaction database.

the Opcenter Execution Core Security Guide.

Field Definition Type
Hostname in 
Certificate                                 
Specify attribute and attribute values in the Hostname in Certificate 
field to be matched against the attribute and attribute values in the 
database electronic certificate to validate the hostname in certificate. 
• Oracle. For Oracle, Hostname in Certificate must match the 
common name (CN) attribute of the  database server certificate 
distinguished name.  Additional certificate attributes may be 
required in the Hostname in Certificate value if the database 
certificate distinguished name was generated with additional 
certificate attributes like Organization, Organizational unit, 
City/locality, State/province.  You must include these additional 
certificate attributes, in addition to the common name 
attribute, in the Hostname in Certificate value if these 
attributes are defined in the database certificate distinguished 
name.  Each attribute must have the attribute abbreviation 
specified followed by an equal sign and the attribute value in 
the Hostname in Certificate value.  Each attribute must be 
separated by a comma.
Example of Hostname in Certificate values in Opcenter EX 
MDD or CR Management Studio.
Only the CN attribute is defined in the database certificate:
CN=oramachine12.opac.local
 
Attributes in addition to the CN attribute are defined in the 
database certificate:
CN=oramachine12.opac.local, C=US
CN=oramachine12.opac.local, O=APAC, OU=IT,
L=Charlotte, S=North Carolina, C=US
• SQL Server. For SQL Server, Hostname in Certificate  must 
match only the common name (CN) portion in the certificate 
distinguished name. Other certificate attributes  like 
Organization, Organizational unit, City/locality, State/province 
may have been entered when the database server certificate 
distinguished name was generated.    These other certificate 
attributes are not required when specifying the Hostname in 
Certificate  value in Opcenter Management Studio, but you can 
specify any or all of them if you like.
Example of Validate Server Certificate values in Opcenter 
Management Studio:
CN=sqlmachine12.opac.local, C=US
CN=sqlmachine12.opac.local
CN=sqlmachine12.opac.local, O=APAC, OU=IT, L=Charlotte, 
S=North Carolina, C=US
Note: The field is required if the  Hostname in Certificate check 
box is marked.

Total Number of 
Sites
Number of  sites that the enterprise will contain. This number 
determines the range of identifiers Siemens will reserve for sites in the 
enterprise. 
10 is a good default for most Siemens customers. You should 
overestimate the number of sites to allow for expansion. 
For example, enter the number of sites + 3 if you expect to have more 
than 10 sites.
Important: This number should not exceed 15. If you need a value 
greater than 15, contact Product Support to discuss this value. 
Required
Site Identifier Number that represents a unique identifier for the site.
Do not assign 0 (zero) because it is reserved for Siemens use. Once a 
DBID is assigned to a site, do not reuse it even after removing the site.
This number should not exceed 15. 
Required

Threshold
Number of identifiers available (out of the more than two thousand 
million possible) at which point the system administrator is notified 
that the system is running out of identifiers.
Keep the default value of 500000.

Chapter1:ManagementStudio
Managing DataStore Database Configuration
You need to configure a DataStore database only if you will be running DataStore. The Metadata and 
Transaction databases must be created first. Be sure to add the DataStore Database Configuration to all 
Application Servers in the environment (Portal and CIO servers).
DataStore Database Configuration  Information
Configuration information for the DataStore database appears in the right pane of the Management Studio 
window when you add or select the DataStore database.
A Databases menu appears on the Management Studio window when the Databases folder or database 
nodes are selected. Refer to "Databases Menu" for information on the Databases menu. 
Note: A warning message pop-up appears when you lower the number of insert tables when the 
number of insert tables is 3 or greater. Click Yes on the pop-up to close the pop-up and reduce 
the number of insert tables. Click No to close the pop-up and discard your changes. 
A toggle switch appears in the DataStore State field section that displays the current state of your 
DataStore database and allows you to start and stop your database. The toggle switch has two states:
• Running, where the toggle switch is set to the right option and displays a green circle with the 
word "on." 
• Stopped, where the toggle switch is set to the left option and displays a red circle with the word 
"off."
A message appears in place of the toggle switch when Management Studio cannot reach the DataStore 
database. Verify your DataStore database connection information to ensure Management Studio can reach 
your database.
Note: You can also start and stop your DataStore database from the Databases menu. The toggle 
switch's state updates to reflect your database's state when you start or stop your database 
from the Databases menu.
Important Caution
During the Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR installation process, several database objects such as 
tables, links, jobs, and sequences are created. Do not modify any of these objects without first consulting 
Product Support, as any changes to the configuration of these objects could cause a DataStore failure or 
data corruption.

DataStore State
Contains a toggle switch with green "on" and red "off"  options that display the current state of your 
DataStore database  and allow you to  start or stop your database.

DataStore Configuration
Cleanup Batch Size 
(1-50000)
Number of Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR transactions that 
are removed in a single database commit cycle if the number of 
processed Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR transactions in the 
queue tables on the Transaction (Production) database exceeds 
this number. The default value is 5000.
Required
Number of Insert 
Tables (2-8)
Number from 2 through 8 for the number of insert tables. The 
default value is 2.
Required
Commit Batch Size 
(0-1000)
Number of Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR transactions to 
batch in a single commit cycle while there are Opcenter EX MDD or 
Opcenter EX CR transactions to be processed. This only applies to 
Oracle databases. A value of 0 means that everyOpcenter EX MDD 
or Opcenter EX CR transaction will be committed in a single batch 
cycle and no batching of Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR 
transactions will be done. The default value is 0 for SQL Server and 
100 for Oracle.
Note: This field is disabled and displays a value 0 if your DataStore 
database type is SQL Server. Switching your server type back to 
Oracle displays the original value you set.
Required

1-34 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
Field Definition Type
Log Level Detail Log Level Detail determines the granularity of log messages logged 
to the DataStoreLog tables. Valid values are Critical Errors, Min 
Detail, and Max Detail. The default value is Critical Errors.
Required
Sleep Time Between 
Polling (2-60)
Number of seconds the DataStore waits between reading the 
queue tables on the Transaction (Production) database. Valid 
values are 2-60. The default value is 2.
Required
Log Retention 
Duration
Amount of time that data is retained in the DataStoreErrors and 
DataStoreLog tables. The default value is 6 months. Valid values 
are Indefinite, 1 Month, 3 Months, 6 Months, 9 Months, 1 Year, 2 
Years, and 3 Years. 
Data is purged from the DataStoreErrors and DataStoreLog table 
that is older than the value specified as the Log Retention Duration.
Required
Retry Attempts (1-
100)
Number of attempts for the DataStore to resolve out of sync 
conditions such as uncommitted Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX 
CR transactions in the Transaction (Production) database or 
transaction sequence gaps in the DataStore database. When the 
Retry Attempts value is exceeded with the out of sync condition 
not resolved, the DataStore stops. Valid values are 1-100. The 
default value is 2.
Required
Verify DB Host Flag with valid values of Yes or No. The default value is No if the 
DataStore is on SQL Server and Yes if the DataStore is on Oracle. 
This flag determines if a comparison is done to verify that the 
DataStore metadata in the DataStore instance matches the 
DataStore executing environment, and that the DataStore and 
OLTP metadata in the OLTP instance matches DataStore and 
OLTP metadata in the DataStore instance. 
Required
E-mail Management
Recipient E-mail E-mail address or list of  e-mail addresses for the recipients of the 
DataStore e-mail alerts to notify the recipients if the DataStore is 
stopped or started.  For Oracle, separate e-mail addresses with a 
comma. For SQL Server, separate e-mail addresses with a 
semicolon.
Optional
Sender E-mail E-mail address of the sender of the DataStore e-mail alerts to notify 
the recipients if the DataStore is stopped or started.  This is 
commonly set to a no reply e-mail address like no-reply_
DataStore@domain.com.
Optional

Managing Services
 The Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR servers are registered as services. You can view the status of 
these services  from the Services pane. You can also start, stop, and restart them from the Services pane.
Services Information
Information for product-related services appears in the right pane of the Management Studio window 
when you select Services from the Administration folder. The right pane contains a table that lists the  
services and their current status.
Sites contain these services by default:
• Camstar App Server
• Camstar Deployment Server
• Camstar Notification Server 
• Camstar Security Services
• Camstar WCF Services
• Portal
• REST API Label
• REST API Modeling
• REST API Query
• REST API Shopfloor
A Services menu appears on the Management Studio window when you select the Services node  in the 
Administration folder. Refer to "Services Menu" for information on the Services menu. 
Important:Starting and stopping  services only affects their status on the currently selected server  
on the Services for <server> field. Selecting another server refreshes the list of  services 
and their current status.
Refer to the Opcenter Execution Medical Device and Diagnostics Installation Guide or the Opcenter 
Execution Core Installation Guide for information on the recommended order to stop and restart services. 
Some of the services run as application pools in IIS and others run as services in Windows Administrative 
Tools.

Verification and Deletion Procedures
These procedures explain how to verify the database connection settings in Management Studio and how 
to delete a database in Management Studio.

database nodes are selected. Refer to "Databases Menu" for information on the Databases 
menu.
How to Verify the Database Connection Settings
Follow these steps to verify the database connection settings:
1. Click Start > Opcenter Execution MDD > Management Studio. The Management Studio 
window appears.
Or
Click Start >  Opcenter Execution Core > Management Studio. The Management Studio win-
dow appears.
2. Select the database for which you want to verify the connection. The configuration information 
for that database appears in the right pane.
3. Click Test Connect. Management Studio displays a "Connection Established" message if the 
connection is successful. Otherwise, Management Studio displays an error message. 
Note: The Test Connect button for the Transaction and DataStore databases validates only the 
user name and the password. It does not validate the schema.

1. Click Start > Opcenter Execution MDD > Management Studio. The Management Studio 
window appears.
Or
Click Start >  Opcenter Execution Core > Management Studio. The Management Studio win-
dow appears. 
2. Right-click the database you want to delete and select Delete Database. A confirmation dialog 
box appears.
3. Click Yes. The database entry is deleted.

=== PDF PAGE 65 ===
Chapter1:ManagementStudio
Creating New Database Tables
The next step after adding the databases in Management Studio is to populate your database 
management system (DBMS) with product-related tables. You can create tables for the Transaction 
database and the DataStore database. 
Note: This topic describes creating database tables for the Transaction database. Refer to the Opcen-
ter Execution Medical Device and Diagnostics DataStore Reference Guide or the Opcenter Exe-
cution Core DataStore Reference Guidefor information on creating tables for the DataStore 
database.  
When Creating New Database Tables
The metadata fields, tables, relationships, instance data, indexes, stored procedures, and primary keys in 
the production database are created by performing the Create New Database Tables task. You perform this 
task after installing Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR for the first time.
The Create New Database Table task is designed to automate these processes:
• Creating product-related tables, database indexes, and stored procedures in your DBMS
• Loading the initial default data in your DBMS, including the default security administration roles 
and permissions 
You can select to load the default Portal seed data if applicable.
SSL security is enabled by default, indicating that the Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR  products and 
modules run in a secure environment. If you have disabled SSL security, you can clear the checkmark from 
the Encrypt Web Traffic check box when generating WCF services. Refer to the Opcenter Execution Medical 
Device and Diagnostics Installation Guide or the Opcenter Execution Core Installation Guide for 
information on SSL security.
A Databases menu appears on the Management Studio window when the Databases folder or database 
nodes are selected. Refer to "Databases Menu" for information on the Databases menu. 
In Clustered Environments
Perform the Create or Update process only once from any member in the cluster in a clustered 
environment.

1-46 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
Configuring Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR
The Configuration pane is used to specify various system parameters.
When Configuring Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR
Prerequisites
These are prerequisites for configuring Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR:
• You must set the Metadata database  before you can specify the default security domain. 
• You must set a default security domain on the Configuration pane before you can start the 
Application Server.
• You must specify a single sign-on method of authentication on the SSO tab.
• You must specify a REST API user name and password if you are using the REST APIs.
Database Connections
Database connections are cached in the Application Server. The connections in the connection pool are 
not released back to the pool as long as the server is actively using the connection.  The only way to clean 
up connections is to stop and start the Application Servers.
Idle database connection cleanup is controlled by two fields in the InSite SiteInfo table in the SiteInfo.mdb. 
Information for these fields is entered in the Configuration pane:
• Enable idle connections cleanup
This check box enables logging of the connection cleanup to the  event log.
• Close connections after being idle for
This field populates the LogDBConnectionCleanup value in the table. The value is in seconds and 
indicates the amount of time a connection must be idle before it is closed.
Note: You may want to enable logging initially so you can verify that connections are being 
cleaned up. You should clear this value to disable logging activity once the database is in 
production.
Notification Server
The  Notification Server is a stand alone .NET-based service. It uses its own configuration file to establish 
connection to the database from which e-mail messages are polled. 
Generated e-mail messages are  stored in a table in the database with the SMTP Server connection 
information. The  Notification Server polls this table for new e-mail messages to send, and then sends the 
messages to the SMTP Server for delivery. 
The  Notification Server includes a retry mechanism for messages that cannot be sent successfully. For 
multiple  Notification Servers that are accessing the same database, a row locking mechanism is provided 
to ensure that the e-mail message is only processed by a single server. 

=== PDF PAGE 71 ===
Chapter1:ManagementStudio
E-mail notification uses the  Notification Server to submit e-mails to the SMTP Server. The Configuration 
settings in Management Studio contain the settings for the  Notification Server. You can modify the 
Configuration settings to change the way the  Notification Server works.
Note: When setting the Sessions parameter in your Oracle database, keep in mind that the Noti-
fication Server takes approximately 100 connections against the database. Refer to  "Sample 
Database Creation Parameters"  for information on the Sessions value.
Permissions Required for a Local Account to Run the  Notification Server Service
Normally, if the  Notification Server account is run under a particular user, that user must be a member of 
the local Administrators group. However, if a non-administrative local account at your site is utilized to run 
the Notification Server service, then a system administrator must set these permissions: 
• For the non-administrative local account, set Read/Write access to the .config files in the directory 
in which the applications reside.
• For the IIS user group, set Read access to the same .config files.
This is also true of the Security Server. 
Application Servers  in a Network Load Balancing (NLB) Cluster 
If your  Application Servers are in an NLB cluster and you have more than two nodes, it is a best practice to 
have the Notification Server running on no more than two of them. The service polls the database every 
second by default to search for new e-mails to send. Contention issues can occur if multiple servers poll 
the same table  frequently. Additionally, each Notification Server instance creates approximately 100 
connections against the database.
Configuring E-Mail Notification Settings for  Quality Records
The "Configuring WIP E-Mail" chapter discusses the configuration of Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR 
to send maintenance and WIP message e-mails when an operator completes a transaction on a resource. 
This is a separate function from the   quality record E-mail notification function.
Configuring License Expiration Notifications
Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR provide the ability to automatically notify designated recipients 
when their  license expires. You can also use Management Studio to configure settings to send e-mail 
notices at intervals before the license expires. There are two intervals you can set:
• Warning Period
• Critical Period
The application sends the e-mail notification once per period: once for warning and once for critical. For 
example, if you set the warning period to 30 days and the critical period to 15 days, you will receive a 
warning notice 30 days before your license expires followed by a critical notice 15 days before your license 
expires. You will also receive an e-mail when the license expires.
When Configuring Inline SPC
Access the License tab and click Save to activate the Inline SPC license   and update the Flexlm license 
server with the Inline SPC value in the appsettings file. Inline SPC provides the ability to automatically save 
displayed charts to a file path located on your server. Access the Inline SPC tab to configure the default file 
path.
Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide 1-47

=== PDF PAGE 72 ===

1-48 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
Note: The Inline SPC tab only appears when Inline SPC is installed. Refer to the Opcenter Execution 
Medical Device and Diagnostics Installation Guide or the Opcenter Execution Core Installation 
Guide.
When Configuring License Expiration Notifications
You can set the Warning Period to zero to disable Warning Period e-mail notifications.
You can set the Critical Period to zero to disable Critical Period e-mail notifications.
Note: The maximum number of days you can enter in the Warning Period and Critical Period fields is 
365.
Separate e-mail recipient addresses with a semicolon. At least one address is required.
The license period expiration date is reset when a new license is issued.
Subject and text for each of the e-mail notifications is system-defined.
You must define an SMTP transport for the Exchange server and a new site that uses the SMTP transport. 
The site name must match the site name used in Management Studio. SMTP transports and sites are 
defined in Modeling.
Temporary licenses are available on an emergency basis through Product Support. For example, if you 
order additional licenses, Siemens can provide temporary licenses until your new license order is 
processed.
Note: The application adds the license server port number (28000/29000), and as a prefix to the 
license server fields after you save configuration information.
Licensing Errors
Certain log on and log off procedures can affect the number of available licenses. Follow this basic 
procedure when logging on to the Portal from your web browser to prevent errors:
1. Start a new session of your web browser.
2. Log on to the Portal.
Refer to the Opcenter Execution Medical Device and Diagnostics Getting Started User Guide or the 
Opcenter Execution Core Getting Started User Guide for information on logging in.
Starting a browser using any of these methods results in a license error. Do not start your browser using 
one of these procedures: 
• Launching a new browser session using the .exe file (such as selecting it from the Start menu)                         
• Opening a new window from an existing browser session
• Launching a new tab from an existing browser session
Siemens does not support multiple instances of the application in the same browser.
Installing a new license without restarting the product-related application pools and services can affect the 
license information displayed in Management Studio. Refer to the Opcenter Execution Medical Device and 
Diagnostics Installation Guide or the Opcenter Execution Core Installation Guide for information on 
installing new license files.

=== PDF PAGE 73 ===
Chapter1:ManagementStudio
Single Sign-On
Using the single sign-on authentication approach, users can access multiple Siemens on-premise or cloud-
based applications by logging into any of the applications. The single sign-on approach provides a single 
identity and access management solution across the full ecosystem of Siemens applications and services.
You must configure single sign-on in Management Studio  to enable single sign-on.
Single sign-on is available in the desktop mode and in the mobile mode.
The log in options are:
• Default - The default log in implementation is logging in without single sign-on. You can continue 
using the existing log in functionality by entering your username and password.
• SAM - The Security and Access Management (SAM) authentication mechanism validates 
authorization as required by Siemens services for access to other Siemens services and resources. 
A unique access key (Webkey) is generated for all account IDs and services, and the SAM service 
validates the authorization as needed to access the other services.
• SAML- Authentication via SAML adapter is extended to Azure Active Directory (Azure AD). 
Refer to "How to Build a SAML Application using the ID Portal (Azure AD)" for more information.
Refer to the Azure AD documentation for more information on this option.
• Windows Authentication - Authentication using your Windows credentials.
• Smart Card - Authentication using smart cards.
Note: Security and Access Management (SAM) authentication is the only single sign-on authen-
tication method available for Siemens cloud-based services.
REST API Settings
REST APIs provide an easy-to-use Web API abstraction layer for executing transactions supported by the 
Application Server. REST APIs enable you to execute transactions more efficiently than if using WCF 
services. The REST APIs are installed by default when you install Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR.

=== PDF PAGE 74 ===
Chapter1:ManagementStudio
1-50 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
Each of the folders listed in the table contains an appsettings.json file. If you want to use the REST APIs, 
you must specify valid credentials (user name and password). You can specify these credentials  on the 
REST API tab. The application encrypts the password you enter, and then passes the user name and 
encrypted password to each of the  appsettings.json files when you click the Save  button.
Note: Entering a user name and encrypted password  manually into each of the appsettings.json files 
is an alternate method for specifying credentials.
By default, the application uses static DLLs for the Modeling, Shopfloor, and Query APIs to enhance 
performance and decrease the amount of time it takes to load the APIs initially. You can set 
UseStaticAssembly to false in the  appsettings.json file if necessary.
Cross-Origin Resource Sharing (CORS)
CORS is a mechanism defined by the Fetch API Specification that allows REST API to specify which origins 
are permitted to access its resources. This is essential for web security because browsers block requests 
from different origins by default for security reasons.
Only requests from origins you specify (for example, https://example.com) are allowed to access the API. If 
you try to access the API from a different origin, the request is denied due to the CORS policy. If an 
application from an unapproved origin attempts to access the API, it receives a CORS error response. This 
prevents unauthorized domains from using your API.
When you specify an origin on the CORS tab and click Save, the application updates the appsettings.json 
file for each REST API service (Modeling, Shopfloor, Query, and Labels). The application writes the 
information to the AllowedOrigins section:
"AllowedOrigins": [
"https://example.com"
]
Note: If you have manually entered information in the AllowedOrigins section of any of the appset-
tings.json files, it will be overwritten by any origins you specify in Management Studio.
From a web application hosted at https://example.com, a GET request to https://{server}/modeling, for 
example, succeeds if you have specified the origin in the allowed list (whitelist) on the CORS tab. A request 
from an unauthorized origin, such as https://unauthorized.com, is blocked by default according to the 
CORS policy.

How to Configure License Expiration Notifications
Follow these steps to configure license expiration notifications:

8. Enter the  e-mail address to be recorded as the E-mail Sender of the notification e-mail.
9. Enter the  list of e-mail addresses, separated by semicolons, to which the e-mail notification will 
be sent.
10. Select the license file type.
11. Click Save. Management Studio displays a success message.

The following key values required during setting up SSO are Siemens generated and should not be 
modified or deleted from the <appSettings> section in the web.config file.
Once configured, the Use Single Sign-On (SSO) button will be displayed on the login page. SSO login 
requests are valid for one minute. Any request exceeding this duration will expire.
Note: If you are using single sign-on authentication for the first time—when changing from the 
Default option to either SAM, SAML etc OR  changes/updates are made either in the con-
figuration details or the SSO authentication method is changed after setting it the first time; 
update the values for the username and password from the Management Studio page. (The cre-
dentials could be of any service account or the Camstar admin user.) Once the values are 
updated, perform an iisreset.
Note: If you log out of the application or are logged out due to session expiration or refreshing the 
application, you will be logged out of the Camstar application only. The single sign-on session 
will remain active and you will not be required to re-enter your credentials when logging back 
in via SSO.
 
Example: Configuring Single Sign-On on a SAML Application created on Azure AD
Follow these steps to configure Single sign-on for a SAML application within Azure AD. 
Note: Please be advised that the configuration of Single Sign-On requires specific URLs and SAML-specific 
considerations, which are available in the metadata of the SAML application you have created. For certain 
steps, the necessary URL details have been hardcoded and must be entered as specified in this document 
in the section, SSO Tab Field Definitions.
The domain should be consistent at the IDP configuration, Management Studio configuration and  the 
browsed URL of the Opcenter application, otherwise it will give a bad request.
1. Select Single sign-on on the SAML application's overview page.

=== PDF PAGE 89 ===
Chapter1:ManagementStudio
2. Click Edit 
  in the Basic SAML Configuration grid. The following two details need to be 
manually entered
1) Entity ID - The SAML entity ID is  unique name. Enter the same Entity ID details 
provided in the Basic SAML Configuration grid in Management Studio > Entity ID/Is-
suer.
2) Assert URL - Provide the domain name and the server name in the default format 
provided in this document- https://{{mydomain}}/IDPAgent/api/v1/sso-service-pro-
vider/saml-assert-consumer-service in Management Studio > Assert URL and in the 
Basic SAML Configuration grid, under Reply URL (Assertion Consumer Service URL).
The Assert URL provided in the SAML application and in the Management Studio portal should be 
the same.
3) Sign On URL (Optional) - Though this is an optional field this detail is required to con-
figure single sign-on correctly. Enter the domain name in this format - https://<mydo-
main>/CamstarPortal/Default.aspx
4) Logout URL (Optional) - This is the Logout Response URL. Use the URL provided in this 
document, https://<mydomain>/IDPAgent/api/v1/sso-service-provider/saml-logout-
response.
3. Once the configurations under Basic SAML are complete, you can proceed with the configurations 
under  Set up (SAML Application)
1) Login URL -  Enter the Login URL provided in the Set up grid of the SAML application in 
Management Studio> Endpoint URL.
2) Logout URL -  Enter the Logout URL provided in the Set up grid of the SAML application 
in Management Studio>Logout URL.

1-68 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
Single Sign-On Setup Script
When SAM or SAML is selected as the SSO option in Management Studio, the SSO Setup button is 
displayed. Using this button you can configure single sign-on by executing an automated script that sets 
up all the necessary URLs for both SAM and SAML. This script ensures faster setup, reduced manual errors, 
consistency across environments and easier replication of configurations. The script configures all 
necessary URLs (e.g., Identity Provider (IdP) metadata URL, Callback URL, Logout URL etc). The script also 
handles certificate uploads and references.
How to configure SSO using the script
1. On the Management Studio page, select either SAM or SAML from the Sign-in Via dropdown.
2. Select the SSO Setup button. A warning is displayed indicating that all the previously configured 
URLs will be overwritten. Click Yes.The Power shell terminal to update the web.config file opens.
3. On the interface you are prompted to enter the Opcenter Admin username and password. Retype 
the password to confirm. 
4. Enter the login method SAML/SAM. Type Y to confirm.
5. Confirm the server name. By default the application selects the name of the server you are 
currently working on. You can either continue with the default selection and type Y, or type N 
and select a different server. Type Y once again to confirm.
6.  Follow the configuration instructions provided in the interface to populate the required URL 
fields for the SAML and SAM parameters listed below:
SAML
• Callback URL - Provided by the script and set to default 
https://mydomain/camstarportal/Default.aspx?mode=classic&theme=Horizon. Type Y to 
confirm.
• External IDP Public Key- Download the IDP Agent security certificate to any folder on your 
machine. Once ready, you'll be asked for the certificate's storage location. Enter the full path 

=== PDF PAGE 93 ===
Chapter1:ManagementStudio
to that folder, confirm with Y, and then provide the certificate's full filename (e.g., 
mycert.cer).
• Protocol:Use the HTTP or HTTPS protocol.
• The URL values for the following parameters can be found in the Azure application.
- Endpoint URL
- Entity ID URL
- Logout URL
• Enter the URLs when prompted and confirm with Y and proceed.
 SAM
To configure SSO with SAM, proceed according to the instructions displayed on the Power shell 
terminal as described in the previous section above when you select the SAML option. The URLs 
for the following parameters can be found on the FDS Console.
- Auth Handler
- Callback URL
- Post Logout URL
- Client ID
• If the check box to enable PKCE security is checked, providing the Client Secret detail is 
optional. You can successfully configure SAM even without providing this input.
7. The system initiates an iisreset and displays the Single Sign-On URL.
8. Press Enter to close the script. The Management Studio application also closes to ensure that all 
the generated URLs are loaded into Management Studio correctly.
9. On reopening Management Studio  the script will have configured the  Assert URL, Logout Request 
URL and the Callback URL , for SAML configurations, and the Redirect URL for SAM configurations. 
These URLs are loaded into Management Studio directly.

Chapter1:ManagementStudio
How to Build a SAML Application using the ID Portal (Azure AD)
Follow these steps to configure a SAML application on Azure ID:
1. Create a new SAML application on the Azure AD portal using the links listed in the table below:
• To  configure the SAML application using Azure AD, use the following links -
 For internal Siemens users- https://wiki.siemens.com/display/en/OneAD+Wiki and navigate 
to the section, "How to onboard an application to Siemens Azure AD single sign-on 
(OIDC/SAML)"
For external users- https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/active-directory/manage-
apps/add-application-portal-setup-sso
Note:  This step populates the value for the External IDP Public Key field.
2. Set the SSO configuration path in the IDP agent, in the appsettings.json file. For  example: 
 "SSOSettingFileName": "SSOConfig.json",
 "SSOSettingFilePath": C:\Program Files (x86)\Camstar\InSite Administration"
3. Download the certificate (the .cert file ) from the IDP portal and save it in the folder where the IDP 
agent is deployed. For example: Navigate to the installation directory where Opcenter Execution 
Core or Opcenter Execution MDD is installed and navigate to \SSO\IdpAgent\app_data\Certificates. 
Create the app_data\Certificates folder if it is not present. 
Note: For Azure AD download the base64 certificate. If running through script, the folder is 
created automatically and  this step can be skipped.
4. A private key is created when user selects SAML in Management Studio that is stored in app_
data/jwt folder. By default, the password is set to 'localhost'. If required, you can retrieve the 
cert.pfx file from app_data/jwt folder.
5.  Copy the text from the IdpSaml.crt certificate,  in the verify certificate section of the SAML 
application.
6. To reset the IIS, run command prompt in the Administrator mode and enter the command, 
C:\WINDOWS\system32\iisreset
7. Once all the configuration details for Management Studio have been generated and updated in 
the fields under SAML you can log into the application using the traditional login URL. For 
example https://localhost/CamstarPortal/Default.aspx
Note:  The session timeout value should not exceed 100 minutes. Update "SessionExpireTime" in the 
appsettings file in IDP Agent and update "SessionState" in the web-config file for Opcenter EX 
CR and Opcenter EX MDD. 
The"SessionState" value that is updated in the web-config file should also be configured in the 
IIS Application Pool. Navigate to IIS > Open Application Pool > Select Portal > Advance Setting > 
Idle Time-out and ensure that the Idle Time-out setting is configured to match the value 
defined for "SessionState."
Note:  While creating or configuring the SAML application on IDP (for example Azure AD), use this 
logout URL to send the SAML response back to the application - https://<mydo-
main>/IDPAgent/api/v1/sso-service-provider/saml-logout-response.

Generating Client ID and Client Secret for SAM 2.0
Follow these steps to generate the Client ID and Client Secret details on the onboarding console, 
Foundational Services (FDS)
1. Ensure that you have access to the FDS onboarding console. On the Onboarding Console page, 
click  Add Application and select Register Application (Advanced).
2. Fill all the required details in the General Information tab and information in the other tabs like 
Tier Details, High Value Add-ons etc.
3. In the Additional Application Configuration tab, select the check boxes, Identity Provider Token 
is sent and Product Provisioning is sent.
4. In the App Credentials tab, update the following details:
1) Grant Type - Authorization Code/Authorization Code with PKCE
2) Name - Any suitable unique identifier
3) Redirect URL- Enter the Redirect URL
Note: When the Authorization Code grant type is selected providing the Client Secret is 
mandatory. However, when Authorization Code with PKCE is selected, providing the 
Client Secret details is optional.
5. Click Configure. The Client ID and Client Secret Details are generated.


=== PDF PAGE 97 ===
Chapter1:ManagementStudio
6. Click the Register App button on the top right corner of the page. The following success 
message is displayed.
Enabling Logging when using SAML
To troubleshoot SAML logs, you can enable logging for the IDP Agent and review the logs in the IDP Agent Log 
folder.
Enabling IDPAgent Logs
Follow the steps outlined below to enable logging for the IDP Agent:
1. Open IIS and navigate to Application Pool.
2. Go to Advanced Settings.
3. Under Application Pool Identity, change from Network Service to LocalSystem.
4. Once logging is enabled, perform an IISReset. Log in again and proceed to check the logs.
5. To access the IDP Agent log path, navigate to IIS and locate the IDPAgent Site.
6. Right-click on the IDPAgent site and select Explore.
7. Check the IDP Agent Log folder to review the logs for any errors.

1-78 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
Updating the Transaction Database
The Update Database task in Management Studio updates your transaction and operational DataStore 
databases assuming the database configurations  are in place. Perform this task after making configuration 
changes in Designer and only after the Create New Database Tables task has been performed. Refer to 
"Creating New Database Tables" for information.
Changes entered through Designer are recorded in the InSite.mdb files. You must apply these changes to 
your transaction database and, if relevant, your DataStore database. Your Transaction and DataStore 
databases must be different databases when performing a database update. The application updates 
tables and columns in the transaction database when the Designer classes are subclassed and fields are 
added. 
Important:Perform the Create or Update process only once from any member in the cluster in a 
clustered environment.
Requirement for Update Database Tasks
The Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR database user requires unrestricted access to these default user 
views for Update Database tasks:
• For Oracle
USER_TABLES
USER_TAB_COLUMNS
USER_CONSTRAINTS
USER_CONS_COLUMNS
• For SQL Server
INFORMATION_SCHEMA.TABLES
INFORMATION_SCHEMA.COLUMNS
INFORMATION_SCHEMA.KEY_COLUMN_USAGE
When to Restart Application Pools and Services after Updating the Database
The application typically does not suspend application pools and services while updating the database. 
This enables the application to continue processing shop floor transactions. These situations  require 
stopping the application pools and services and then restarting them after updating the database:
• Regenerating WCF services requires a recycle of the Portal and Camstar WCF Services application 
pools.
• Regenerating REST API services requires a recycle of the Portal and REST API application pools.
• Modifying the database connection information requires a restart of the Camstar Security Services 
and Camstar App Server application pools.

=== PDF PAGE 103 ===
Chapter1:ManagementStudio
• Manually changing the database schema  requires a restart of the Camstar App Server application 
pool.
Note: A message stating to restart the Camstar App Server may appear in Management Studio if you 
perform other actions that require restarting. Refer to "Managing Services" for information on 
application pools and services.

How to Update the Transaction Database
Follow these steps to update the transaction database after making changes in Designer: 
1. Click Start > Opcenter Execution MDD > Management Studio. The Management Studio 
window appears.
Or
Click Start >  Opcenter Execution Core > Management Studio. The Management Studio win-
dow appears. 
2. Expand Databases, and then right-click Transaction Database.
3. Select Update Database. The Update Database dialog box appears.
4. Select or clear the update options according to your business requirements. Refer to the check 
box definitions table for information on the check boxes.
5. Click OK. The progress line indicates that the process is running and a SQL Script pop-up 
appears, showing the progress of the scripts as they execute. If an error is encountered, the Error 
Information section is enabled and populated. If you select a different file, the Error Information 
section updates with the Script name.
6. Do one of the following if an error occurs:
• Click the View… button to open the script file in Notepad (or the associated program).
• Click the Run Scripts button to re-run all scripts from the beginning.

Chapter1:ManagementStudio
Uploading and Assigning a Custom Dictionary
You can create custom dictionaries to organize your custom or translated  system and user labels. You can 
assign dictionaries to employees on an individual basis.
When Uploading and Assigning a Custom Dictionary
You can upload dictionary labels using custom .csv files in Management Studio. The application displays 
the appropriate labels contained in the dictionary when an employee with a designated dictionary logs 
into Portal. If an employee does not have a designated dictionary, the default labels appear.
Dictionary labels are cached in both Application Server and Portal application caches. Dictionary labels are 
stored within the database and cached in Portal's IIS cache. You must recycle the IIS application pool to 
refresh the cache. The dictionary label cache is updated when a dictionary modeling instance is updated. 
On saving of a dictionary Modeling instance, the label cache is automatically refreshed out of the box if 
the save includes changes. 
Refer to the Opcenter Execution Medical Device and Diagnostics Modeling User Guide or the Opcenter 
Execution Core Modeling User Guide for information on defining dictionaries. Refer to the Opcenter 
Execution Medical Device and Diagnostics Designer User Guide or the Opcenter Execution Core Designer 
User Guide for information on labels.
How to Upload a Custom Dictionary
Follow these steps to upload a custom dictionary:
1. Export the labels file. 
2. Ensure the spreadsheet is formatted correctly.  The following columns are required:  LabelID, 
Name, CategoryID, LabelValue, WorkspaceCode, and NewValue.  
Note: The last column is named Column1 when it is exported, so the column must be 
renamed to NewValue.
3. Enter the translations of each entry into the NewValue column.
4. Save the spreadsheet as a .csv file using the name you want to use as your Dictionary modeling 
object. 
5. Copy the .csv file to:  C:\Program Files (x86)\Camstar\InSite Administration\Dictionaries.
6. Click Start > Opcenter Execution MDD > Management Studio. The Management Studio 
window appears.
Or
Click Start >  Opcenter Execution Core > Management Studio. The Management Studio win-
dow appears.
7. Expand Databases, and then right-click Transaction Database.
8. Select Update Database. The Update Database dialog box appears.
9. Select Load Camstar Dictionaries and Reload All.
10. Click OK. The dictionary modeling object and associated translations are created.

Chapter1:ManagementStudio
Enabling Server Trace Logs
You can enable different levels of server trace logging from the <Server Name> - Logs menu. Server traces 
allow you to capture debugging information for various requests and responses.
When Enabling Server Trace Logs
You enable Server trace logs by selecting a server node from the Camstar Servers folder and then selecting 
a server trace log from the <Server Name> - Logs menu. Refer to "<Server Name> -  Logs Menu" for 
information on the <Server Name> - Logs menu.
Note:  You must re-enable the Server trace logs after completing a database update (dbupdate). Re-
enabling the logs is required because the server is restarted and server trace logs are disabled 
when a dbupdate is performed. 
This table defines what information is captured at each server trace log level.
Level Information captured
Low Function names and parameters.
Med Function names, parameters, and resolved values.
High Function names, parameters, resolved values, and XML requests and responses.
Only one of the three server trace log levels (Low, Med, or High) can be active on a server at a time. For 
example, selecting Low, and then selecting Med would only enable the medium level server trace log. You 
can run separate server traces at different levels on each of your servers simultaneously.  
You can disable an active server trace log by selecting a server node currently running a server trace and 
then selecting Disable Server Trace logs from the Logs menu.
Log files are stored at Program Files (x86)\Camstar\Logs\Transaction Logs by default. Refer to "Reading 
Trace Files" for information on viewing captured logs.
You can also enable a client trace log by selecting Enable Client Trace logs from the <Server Name> - Logs 
menu. The client trace log captures the WCF submit and response documents generated by the client. You 
can then disable the client trace log by selecting Disable Client Trace logs from the <Server Name> - Logs 
menu.
An Open File - Security Warning pop-up may appear when you enable a server trace log. Click Run to 
enable the server trace log and close the pop-up. Click Cancel to cancel the server trace log and close the 
pop-up. Error pop-ups may appear if you cancel your server trace log from the Open File - Security Warning 
pop-up. Click OK to close any Error pop-ups that appear. 
You can optionally add the path to the server trace log executable file to your local intranet settings to 
avoid displaying the Open File - Security Warning pop-up. Add this path to your local intranet settings, 
replacing <hostname> with the hostname or IP address of your Application server:
file://<hostname>\c$\Program Files (x86)\Camstar\InSite Server

additional licenses.

most current number of licenses in use, simply navigate away from the License Information page and then 
redisplay the License Information page.

exceeds the number of available licenses.
Temporary licenses are available on an emergency basis through Product Support. For example, if you 
order additional licenses, Siemens can provide temporary licenses until your new license order is 
processed.  Refer to the Opcenter Execution Medical Device and Diagnostics Installation Guide or the 
Opcenter Execution Core Installation Guide for information on installing new licenses.

Managing Performance Counters
You can manage performance counters  for your sites to capture performance information  in 
Management Studio.
Performance Counters Information
Performance counter  information appears in the right pane of the Management Studio window when you 
select the Performance Counters  node from the Administration folder. The performance counter within 
Management Studio collects raw data from one counter at a time based on the counter selected. The 
application saves performance counter logs as CSV files. The default file name is PerformanceCounter.csv. 
The default location for the saved logs is C:\Program Files (x86)\Camstar\Logs.
The application overwrites the PerformanceCounter.csv file every time you start a performance counter.
The performance logs contain column header information. The first column is DateTimestamp. 
DateTimestamp includes the date and time the sample was collected. Three additional column headers 
may be included based on your selected performance counter settings:
• Category
• Counter
• Instance
Each performance counter setting header is separated by "[]" and "." in the name.
Examples:
DateTimestamp,[category].[counter].[instance]
DateTimestamp,[ASP.NET Applications].[% Managed Processor Time Base (estimated)].[__Total__]
Performance counters  use the Microsoft Performance Monitor and Performance Counters. Refer to 
Microsoft's Support site for information on the Microsoft Performance Monitor and Performance Counters.
The application displays an error message when an error occurs. Errors are logged in the Event Viewer.

Chapter1:ManagementStudio
Managing Workspaces
Siemens provides workspaces for metadata to make development, deployment, and upgrading of partner 
offerings and customer customizations easier and more manageable. Metadata records are assigned to a 
customer, partner,  industry solution, or base  (csi) workspace.
Note: Metadata databases must be converted following an upgrade to Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter 
EX CR  to enable proper workspace functionality. 
Understanding Workspaces
Workspaces provide the ability for customers, partners, and Siemens to change or customize Opcenter EX 
MDD or Opcenter EX CR without overwriting changes made by others. Making changes in a workspace 
isolates those changes from any updates made to any other workspace. For example, changes made in the 
highest level workspace do not affect the base   workspace. Similarly, if a customer accepts an update to 
the base  workspace, customizations in their workspace are not overwritten.
Siemens provides  four types of workspaces:
• Base (csi)
• Industry solution
• Partner
• Customer - (Corporate, Division, and Site workspaces)
The active workspace with the highest sequence number takes priority and is where all work and user 
content  is saved. The  Site workspace is the highest priority workspace.  Once you begin working in a 
workspace in the Designer, you cannot deactivate it.
When Managing Workspaces
The base  and Site  workspaces are active by default. You can activate additional workspaces through 
Management Studio.
You can also make workspaces inactive with the exception of the base workspace. You cannot make the 
base  workspace inactive. Do not make the Site  workspace inactive.  
Customers should always work in the Site  workspace (200). If a customer  deactivates the Site  workspace 
and works only in the base (csi) workspace, any changes made to the base workspace are overwritten 
when the customer installs a software update.
You must update your Transaction database, including generating the WCF services, after making 
workspaces active or inactive.
 You cannot activate or deactivate workspaces if the InSite.mdb file is set to Read Only. 
Management Studio displays a warning message when attempting to manage workspaces if the 
InSite.mdb file is Read Only.
Note: A warning message appears when activating workspace 220. Activating workspace 220 and 
saving changes will prevent any future changes in workspace 200. 
Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide 1-99

completes.
Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide 1-103

=== PDF PAGE 128 ===
Chapter1:ManagementStudio
1-104 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
Managing Workspace JavaScript and CSS Files
You can store custom JavaScript and CSS files for each active workspace which allows custom code to be 
extended to sequential higher workspaces. The application works through each active workspace, 
excluding the base csi workspace, and searches files for duplicate functions in your code files before 
bundling the higher active workspace functionality.
When Managing Workspace JavaScript and CSS Files
You must add a workspace subfolder at the expected file paths when storing custom workspace JavaScript 
and CSS files. Workspace subfolders must  be named the code of the associated workspace. For example, 
the subfolder associated with workspace 200 must be  named "200."
The expected file paths for workspace subfolders are:
• Camstar\Camstar Portal\Scripts\User for JavaScript files.
• Camstar\Camstar Portal\Themes\User for CSS files.
Only one user code file can exist in each workspace subfolder. You must follow the naming convention 
"WorkspaceCode.js" and "WorkspaceCode.css," where "WorkspaceCode" is the workspace's code, such as 
"180" or "190". For example, "200.css" is an accepted file name for a CSS file, and must be placed in the 
"200" subfolder in the Camstar\Camstar Portal\Themes\User folder.
Bundling of custom code files occurs when the server starts. Siemens recommends stopping and restarting 
your servers when adding or updating workspace code files.
Managing Workspace JavaScript and CSS Files Example
In this example, workspaces  190 and 200 are currently active. You have JavaScript files for both 
workspaces and a CSS file for the 200 workspace. These steps outline the correct way to store these files:
1. Navigate to the file path Camstar\Camstar Portal\Scripts\User.
2. Create a new folder named 190.
3. Create another new folder named 200.
4. Rename your JavaScript file for workspace 190 as 190.js and copy it to the newly created 190 
folder.
5. Rename your JavaScript file for workspace 200 as 200.js and copy it to the newly created 200 
folder.
6. Navigate to the file path Camstar\Camstar Portal\Themes\User.
7. Create a new folder named 200.
8. Rename your CSS file for workspace 200 as 200.css and copy it to the newly created 200 folder.
The application bundles the stored files when the server starts. In this example, the application bundles 
the 200.css file and looks for duplicate functionality in the 190.js and 200.js folders, and then bundles 
duplicate functionality found in the 200.js file over the 190.js file, because the 200.js file is associated with 
the highest active workspace.

=== PDF PAGE 129 ===
Chapter1:ManagementStudio
Managing Workspace App Code Files
Siemens provides a MergeAppCode executable to merge app code files across your active workspaces. 
When Managing Workspace App Code Files
The MergeAppCode executable (MergeAppCode.exe) works through each workspace subfolder and 
changes the inheritance on each app code file to inherit from the workspace above it. For example, if the 
csi, 180, and 190 workspaces are all active, then the MoveStd_190 inheritance would change from 
":MoveStd" to ":MoveStd_180." The executable is available at the file path C:\Program Files 
(x86)\Camstar\Insite Conversion Tool\MergeAppCode.
A configuration file (MergeAppCode.exe.config) for modifying the executable is available at the file path 
C:\Program Files (x86)\Camstar\Insite Conversion Tool\MergeAppCode. You can modify the WebPortletPath 
key with the relative or absolute path to the WebPortlet folder.
Important:Do not alter the mdbConnectionString key in the MergeAppCode.config file.
You must add workspace subfolders at the file path C:\Program Files (x86)\Camstar\Camstar Portal\App_
Code\WebPortlets\Workspaces to store your app code files for merging. Workspace subfolders must  be 
named the code of the associated workspace. For example, the subfolder associated with workspace 200 
must be named "200."
You must append all custom controls, functions, and property names with the associated workspace code. 
For example, a control named "ControlName" in workspace 200 must be named "ControlName_200" to 
allow the executable to determine the associated workspace and inheritance. All files must be stored in 
their associated workspace subfolder.
An error message appears if no active workspaces are available, excluding the base csi workspace, or if the 
WebPortletPath cannot be found. Errors are logged in the Event Viewer.
How to Merge App Code Files
Follow these steps to merge app code files:
1. Navigate to the file path C:\Program Files (x86)\Camstar\Insite Conversion Tool\MergeAppCode.
2. Double-click MergeAppCode.exe. A Command Prompt pop-up appears as the executable 
merges the app code files and displays a success message when finished.
Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide 1-105
