Chapter1:ManagementStudio
1-106 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
Managing Workspace Indexes
Indexes defined in Designer are created by a stored procedure that reads in the definition and creates the 
database indexes. There are some cases where changes to an existing index are required but not 
supported by Designer, such as clustered indexes or where additional columns are required for a specific 
workspace. To support these cases, workspace specific stored procedures can be written to be called 
during the index creation process. This allows for modification of existing indexes. Each active workspace 
can have its own procedure, and the index creation process calls each one from your lowest to highest 
active workspace.
When Managing Workspace Indexes
 A stored procedure is executed when updating the Transaction database or creating database tables with 
the Create Indices check box selected. The csiCreateIndexes stored procedure is executed to read in the 
definitions and create indexes based on those definitions. 
After the indexes are created for each active workspace in ascending order according to the workspace 
sequence, the procedure checks for the existence of a stored procedure with a specific name and calls it if 
it exists. For example, if you have workspaces 180, 190, and 200 active with a custom stored procedure 
file for each workspace, the application would execute the  procedures in the order  180, 190, and then 
200.
Note: Any changes made to indexes are logged in the Replace Workspace Data utility's log file. Refer 
to the Opcenter Execution Medical Device and Diagnostics Installation Guide or the Opcenter 
Execution Core Installation Guide for information on the Replace Workspace Data utility.
Only one stored procedure can exist for each active workspace. You must follow the naming convention 
"Workspace_WSCode_CreateIndexes" where "WSCode" is the workspace's code, such as "180" or "190". For 
example, "Workspace_200_CreateIndexes" must be the name for workspace 200’s stored procedure.
Sample stored procedure files are provided for both SQL Server and Oracle databases as a starting point for 
creating your custom index stored procedures. The sample files are available in these locations:
• C:\Program Files (x86)\Camstar\Insite Administration\Scripts\Oracle for Oracle databases.
• C:\Program Files (x86)\Camstar\Insite Administration\Scripts\SqlServer for SQL databases.
You must execute the scripts you created for your database in Management Studio to create the stored 
procedure. A stored procedure is executed when updating the Transaction database or creating database 
tables with the Create Indices check box selected. The csiCreateIndexes procedure then finds the custom 
procedure and calls it during the Index Creation process.

Chapter1:ManagementStudio
Configuring Industry Solutions
The Industry Solutions Configuration Tool enables you to configure the workspaces necessary to run the 
industry solution features. Files needed for the industry solution features are provided as part of Opcenter 
Execution MDD or Opcenter Execution CR , but you must configure them using the tool. You must run the 
tool if you want to use any of the following features:
• CIO 
• Opcenter EX CR Scheduling
• Overall Equipment Effectiveness (OEE)
• Medical Device
• Batch Processing
• Shop Floor Resource Configuration (SRC)
The Industry Solutions Configuration Tool provides a mechanism to deliver CIO functionality that enables 
you to send messages outbound from Opcenter Execution MDD or Opcenter Execution CR to external 
applications.
The industry solutions workspaces provide a mechanism to easily deliver features specific to the medical 
device industry. Features are developed in the industry solutions workspaces and are therefore isolated. 
This enables the medical device-specific functionality to remain separate while still allowing the 
inheritance of subsequent Opcenter Execution MDD or Opcenter Execution CR changes.
 You must be licensed for Opcenter Execution Core Batch Processing to use the Batch Processing features. 
You must be licensed for Opcenter EX CR Scheduling to use the Opcenter EX CR  Scheduling features.
Refer to the Opcenter Execution Medical Device and Diagnostics Installation Guide or the Opcenter 
Execution Core Installation Guide for information on installing Opcenter Execution MDD or Opcenter 
Execution CR, and its components.
Refer to the following guides for information specific to installing, configuring, and using these features:
• Opcenter Execution Medical Device and Diagnostics Scheduling User Guide or the Opcenter 
Execution Core Scheduling User Guide
• Opcenter Execution Medical Device and Diagnostics Industry Solutions User Guide or the 
Opcenter Execution Core Industry Solutions User Guide
• Opcenter Execution Medical Device and Diagnostics Medical Device User Guide or the Opcenter 
Execution Core Medical Device User Guide
• Opcenter Execution Medical Device and Diagnostics Batch Processing User Guide or the Opcenter 
Execution Core Batch Processing User Guide
• Opcenter Execution Medical Device and Diagnostics Modeling User Guide or the Opcenter 
Execution Core Modeling User Guide
Before Configuring Industry Solutions
A DataStore database must be configured  if you are installing Overall Equipment Effectiveness. Refer to 
"Managing DataStore Database Configuration" for information on DataStore databases.

=== PDF PAGE 133 ===
Chapter1:ManagementStudio
The Industry Solutions Configuration Tool performs the following after you make your selections and click 
Finish:
1. Displays the  Confirm Installation dialog box which indicates information such as the install type  
and selected feature workspaces. You can click OK to proceed with the installation or cancel.
2. Verifies that the correct version  of Opcenter Execution MDD or Opcenter Execution CR   is installed  
Note: The tool returns an error if the correct version is not installed. 
3. Indicates that CIO is required if you selected Industry or Batch Processing
Note: You must select CIO to continue with the installation. The tool does not indicate 
that CIO is required if you select Medical Device only.
4. Prompts you to enter DataStore database details if you select OEE
Note: The tool returns an error if you do not enter all details. The Industry workspace is 
required for the OEE option. The tool returns an error if you do not select the 
Industry workspace. The tool verifies that the connection to the DataStore database 
is successful. The tool returns an error if you do not provide the correct connection 
information.
5. Runs the Replace Workspace Data utility for each workspace you selected
6. Copies the .js, .css, and .cs files for each selected workspace to the appropriate locations in the 
\Camstar\Industry Solutions\<feature>\Camstar Portal folder
7. Updates the settings.xml file with the CDOForms settings for each selected workspace
8. Generates the WCF services
9. Moves the virtual pages .xml files for each selected workspace to the 
InsiteAdministration\PortalData folder
10. Performs a database update (dbupdate)
11. Imports Portal forms for each selected workspace
12. Executes menu definition and roles scripts for each selected workspace
13. Restarts the CamstarAppServer and CamstarSecurityServices application pools
14. Executes the Industry Solutions IPL integration configuration scripts if Industry is selected and the 
required data is entered
15. Executes Industry Solutions OEE scripts if you select OEE
A progress bar appears on the Industry Solutions Configuration Tool dialog box to indicate the status of 
the configuration. The tool displays a dialog box when the process finishes.
You can use the industry solution features after the configuration completes. Opcenter EX CR Scheduling 
requires you to install and configure Preactor. Refer to the Opcenter Execution Medical Device and 
Diagnostics Scheduling User Guide or the Opcenter Execution Core Scheduling User Guide for information.

Configuring Shop Floor Resource Configuration (SRC) in Industry Solutions 
The Shop Floor Resource Configuration (SRC) functionality for managing the IPL Smart Settings is installed 
using the Industry Solutions Configuration Tool.  For the SRC to be fully functional, the Valor MSS Shop 
Floor Application Server must also be installed and configured.
Note: If you are upgrading from the current release or installing an update release to the current ver-
sion, fewer dialog boxes appear when you run the installer. The interface guides you through 
the upgrade process.
Before Configuring SRC Installation
SRC requires ASP.NET Core 8.x Windows Hosting Bundle and .NET Desktop Runtime 8.x to be installed.  If 
they are not yet installed, a dialog box will be displayed to prompt if you want to download and install 
ASP.NET Core 8.x Windows Hosting Bundle and .NET  Desktop Runtime 8.x support.  You will be directed to 
the Microsoft site to download the installer for .NET Desktop Runtime 8.x after clicking the OK button.  The 
SRC installation will not proceed if you select No on the dialog box.  Once .NET Desktop Runtime 8.x has 
been installed, you will need to re-open the Industry Solutions Configuration Tool and re-select SRC to 
complete the installation. It is possible to proceed with SRC Installation without installing the ASP.NET Core 
8.x Windows Hosting Bundle, but SRC cannot function without it, so it is absolutely necessary to install the 
ASP.NET Core 8.x Windows Hosting Bundle.
Refer to the Release Matrix on Siemens Support Center for information on the supported version for the 
Hosting Bundle.

Configuring Inline SPC
Inline Statistical Process Control (SPC) enables manufacturing and engineering to configure and include 
SPC control charts in a Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR implementation. Control charts can be 
updated as quality data is collected, and can be displayed to the operator for immediate feedback. 
Engineering can also review historical charts as a part of research into specific events.
When Configuring Inline SPC
Inline SPC provides the ability to automatically save displayed charts to a file path located on your server. A 
default file path (C:\ProgramData\Camstar\InlineSPC\Chart Output) is created during Inline SPC installation. 
You can change the path as needed. Additional options for saving charts are defined on the SPC Chart 
modeling page. Refer to the Opcenter Execution Medical Device and Diagnostics Inline SPC Reference 
Guide or the Opcenter Execution Core Inline SPC Reference Guide.
The Inline SPC tab only appears when Inline SPC is installed. Refer to the Opcenter Execution Medical 
Device and Diagnostics Installation Guide or the Opcenter Execution Core Installation Guide.
Inline SPC Licensing
Inline SPC licensing is enabled on the configuration License tab. Access the License tab and click Save to 
activate the Inline SPC license and update the Flexlm license server with the Inline SPC value in the 
appsettings file.

Encrypting the REST API Password
REST APIs provide an easy-to-use Web API abstraction layer for executing transactions supported by the 
Application Server. REST APIs enable you to execute transactions more efficiently than if using WCF 
services. The Encryption Utility enables you to encrypt the password you use with REST API  if you want to 
specify your credentials manually. After the utility encrypts the password, you can copy it into to the 
appsettings.json file for each API service manually.
Note: Instead of encrypting the password using this utility and then manually copying the encrypted 
password into the appsettings.json files, you can specify your REST API credentials in Man-
agement Studio and have Management Studio automatically encrypt the password and copy 
the credentials to the appsettings.json file for each API service. Refer to "REST API Settings" for 
information.
Refer to the Opcenter Execution Medical Device and Diagnostics Technical Reference: REST API or the 
Opcenter Execution Core Technical Reference: REST API for information on installing and using REST APIs.
Encryption Utility
This image shows an example of the Encryption Utility.
How to Encrypt the REST API Password
Follow these steps to encrypt the REST API password:
1. Click Start > Opcenter Execution MDD > Management Studio. The Management Studio 
window appears.
Or
Click Start >  Opcenter Execution Core > Management Studio. The Management Studio win-
dow appears.
2. Select Encrypt Utility from the Tools menu. The Encrypt Utility window appears.
3. Enter the REST API password you want to use in the Input field.

Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide 2-1
Chapter 2: Metadata Databases
 
Introduction
This chapter discusses the purpose of each database, provides some sample images from the databases, 
and describes the fields in Management Studio that are related to information in the databases. It also 
provides information on the two metadata databases that are installed with Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter 
EX CR:
• InSite.mdb - Contains metadata information specific to Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR. The 
data source name for this database is  Designer.
• SiteInfo.mdb - Contains site-specific connection information. The data source name for this 
database is InSite Site Info.
The metadata databases are configured in Management Studio, explained in Chapter 1. 
Configurations such as object model definitions, entered through the Designer, are written to the 
metadata databases, which contain the definitions for items such as  database tables, columns, 
relationships, keys, indexes and joins.
Most of the configurations are written to InSite.mdb, while a small portion devoted to site-specific 
definitions are stored in SiteInfo.mdb. These databases are portable and can be maintained in Designer on 
any  server running the Microsoft Windows operating system that is supported for this version. 
The Opcenter Meta Link application enables you to get meta models of the OC EX CR or OC EX MDD 
applications in Entity Relationship Diagram (ERD) form to understand entities and their relationships better 
and to help in extending the model during customization. Opcenter Meta Link generates an ERD by 
reading metadata from MDB files and assigning referential integrity relationships for the data 
objects/database tables within the file. You can select the type of output you want to generate based on 
database such as MSSQL data definition language, Oracle data definition language, Microsoft Access 
database, CSV file, and Hierarchy view. Refer to the Opcenter Meta Link Utility document for information.
In This Chapter
This chapter contains these topics:
• Database Creation and Update Processes
• Configuration Entries

=== PDF PAGE 152 ===
Chapter2:MetadataDatabases
2-2 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
Database Creation and Update Processes
This section describes the database creation and update processes.
Creating New Database Tables
Information in the metadata databases is initially released to the production database and activated 
through the Create New Database Tables task in Management Studio, performed after installing Opcenter 
EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR for the first time.
The Create New Database Tables task is designed to automate two processes:
• Creating Camstar tables, database indexes, and stored procedures in your DBMS
• Loading the initial default data in your DBMS
Updating the Databases
Changes made to the factory model using Designer are recorded in InSite.mdb. Following that process, 
you need to propagate the changes to the Transaction database, Siteinfo.mdb, and if applicable, to the 
DataStore database. The Update Database task in Management Studio updates your transaction and 
operational databases. 
Utilizing Mass Updates
You can utilize a mass update setup for performing transactions on large sets of data, such as when 
processing a large number of containers or one large batch transaction. Contact Product Support via the 
Support Center for assistance configuring Designer to utilize mass updates.
Designer Database
The Designer database, InSite.mdb, contains metadata information specific to Opcenter EX MDD or 
Opcenter EX CR. The location of InSite.mdb is specified for the site on the Metadata Database 
Configuration window in Management Studio.
SiteInfo Database
The SiteInfo database table, SiteInfo.mdb, contains site-specific connection information. Like the other 
Designer database, InSite.mdb, its location is specified for the site on the Metadata Database 
Configuration window in Management Studio.
Important:Do not manually alter any value in the .mdb or database except at the written request of 
Product Support.

Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide 3-1
Chapter 3: Configuring WIP E-Mail
 
Introduction
You can configure Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR to send maintenance and WIP message e-mails 
when an operator completes a transaction on a resource. For example, if the operator performs a MoveIn 
transaction on a resource that is due for maintenance, you can configure the application to send a 
“maintenance due” e-mail to a recipient in charge of that resource’s maintenance.
Additionally, you can configure the application to send e-mails when a user performs other transactions, 
for example when the operator performs a MoveStd from specific locations.
In This Chapter
This chapter contains these topics:
• Setting Up the SendEmail Function
• Setting Up Maintenance-Related E-mails
• Setting Up WIP Message E-mails
• Customizing the SendEmail Function

=== PDF PAGE 156 ===
Chapter3:ConfiguringWIPE-Mail
3-2 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
Setting Up the SendEmail Function
To configure the system to send e-mails, you must define an SMTP Transport and at least one e-mail 
notification. The SMTP Transport definition specifies the mail server that the application uses to send the 
e-mails. The E-Mail notification definition is the e-mail address of the recipient to whom the application 
sends the e-mail. (You specify the recipient when setting up the maintenance or WIP-related e-mails.) 
The following topics describe defining an SMTP transport  and an e-mail notification. Refer to the Opcenter 
Execution Medical Device and Diagnostics Modeling User Guide and Opcenter Execution Core Modeling 
User Guide for detailed information on defining SMTP transports and e-mail notifications.
Important:Although you are defining an SMTP Transport, the application does not require the SMTP 
Virtual Host to be installed on the Application Server. 
How to Define an SMTP Transport
Follow these steps to define an SMTP Transport:
1. Log on to the Portal. 
2. Select Modeling from the Modeling menu. The Modeling page appears.
3. Click SMTP Transport. The SMTP Transport page appears within the Modeling page.
4. Click New. Blank fields appear for you to define a new instance.
5. Enter the  name of the SMTP transport in the SMTP Transport field. 
6. Enter optional information according to your business requirements. Refer to the field definitions 
tables for information on the optional fields.
7. Click Save. The application saves the modeling object and displays a success message.
How to Define an E-Mail Notification
Follow these steps to define an E-mail Notification:
1. Log on to the  Portal. 
2. Select Modeling from the Modeling menu. The Modeling page appears.
3. Click E-mail Notification. The E-mail Notification page appears within the Modeling page.
4. Click New. Blank fields appear for you to define a new instance.
5. Enter information in the E-Mail Notification and Recipient fields.
6. Enter optional information according to your business requirements. Refer to the field definitions 
tables for information on the optional fields.
7. Click Save. The application saves the modeling object and displays a success message.
 

=== PDF PAGE 157 ===
Chapter3:ConfiguringWIPE-Mail
Setup Troubleshooting Tip
If your E-mail notifications setup does not work, perform this SQL statement on the EMAILQUEUE table:
Select LastErrorMessage from EMAILQUEUE table;
The response provides  an indication of what is wrong with your setup. 
Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide 3-3

=== PDF PAGE 158 ===
Chapter3:ConfiguringWIPE-Mail
3-4 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
Setting Up Maintenance-Related E-mails
To configure the application to send maintenance-related e-mails, you must define one or more of the 
following.
Thruput Requirements
A Thruput Requirement defines the maximum number of units that a resource can process between 
maintenance events before the next maintenance is due. Thruput requirement schedules include 
allowances for a tolerance quantity and a warning quantity.
Date Requirements
A Date Requirement defines a specific date on which the next maintenance event is to occur on a 
Resource. Date requirement schedules include allowances for a tolerance period and a warning period.
Recurring Date Requirements
A Recurring Date Requirement defines a maintenance schedule for a Resource based on recurring calendar 
dates. Recurring date requirement schedules include allowances for a tolerance period and a warning 
period.
The schedule specifies one or more dates on which a maintenance event must occur, and each date must 
occur independently of when the previous maintenance was completed. For example, if maintenance is 
scheduled to occur every other week on Tuesday, but it was performed two days late on a Thursday, the 
next maintenance event is still scheduled to occur on a Tuesday.
Note: The application will look for the local site when checking for the e-mail transport.  In order for 
emails to send properly, it is important to have a site defined that has the exact same name as 
the site that has been configured in Management Studio. 
Refer to the Opcenter Execution Medical Device and Diagnostics Modeling User Guide or the Opcenter 
Execution Core Modeling User Guide for  information on creating these definitions.

=== PDF PAGE 159 ===
Chapter3:ConfiguringWIPE-Mail
Setting Up WIP Message E-mails
You can configure a WIP message to send e-mail to someone other than the operator (for example, an 
engineer, or a supervisor) when a container reaches a certain step in the production process. For example, 
you might create a WIP message that sends an e-mail to a supervisor when a container is placed on hold.
Refer to the topic on WIP messages in the Opcenter Execution Medical Device and Diagnostics Modeling 
User Guide or the Opcenter Execution Core Modeling User Guide for information on defining WIP 
messages and configuring them to send e-mails. 
Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide 3-5

=== PDF PAGE 160 ===
Chapter3:ConfiguringWIPE-Mail
3-6 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
Customizing the SendEmail Function
You can customize the application to send e-mails when a user performs other transactions. For example, 
you can configure the application to send e-mail messages when the operator performs a MoveStd from 
specific locations.
How to Add a Custom CLF Configuration
Follow these steps to add a custom CLF configuration:
1. Log in to Designer.
2. Click the Logic Flows (CLFs) button. Designer displays the CLFs.
3. Select the UserFunctions node.
4. Click Add. Designer adds a new logic flow to the UserFunctions node.
5. Enter the name of the new logic flow. Enter csi_SendEmail for  example.
6. Click the  General tab and enter a description of the new logic flow.
7. Click the Functions tab.
8. Locate the SendEmail function in the Available functions list.
9. Drag the SendEmail function to the Logic flow list.
10. Leave the parameters. 
11. Perform the "How to Add the MoveStdEmail CDO Subclass" procedure.
How to Add the MoveStdEmail CDO Subclass
Follow these steps to add the CDO subclass, MoveStdEmail:
1. Click the Object definitions (CDOs) button in Designer.
2. Find the MoveStd CDO.
3. Right-click MoveStd and select Add Subclass from the menu that appears. The Create a new 
CDO Definition window appears.
4. Enter MoveStdEmail in the New CDO Name field.
5. Enter a description of the CDO in the Description field.
6. Enter optional information according to your business requirements.
7. Click Create. Designer adds the MoveStdEmail CDO as a subclass of the MoveStd CDO.
8. Create these fields in the MoveStdEmail CDO: 
• Recipient (string) 
• Sender (string) 
• Subject (string) 
• Message (string) 
• EmailTransport (dataTransport) 

=== PDF PAGE 161 ===
Chapter3:ConfiguringWIPE-Mail
Note: Verify that the string fields are long enough to accept the values you want. 
9. Perform the "How to Assign the User CLF to an Event" procedure.
How to Assign the User CLF to an Event
Follow these steps to assign the user CLF to an Event:
1. Ensure that you have the MoveStdEmail CDO selected and click the Events tab. Designer displays 
a list of events associated with the CDO.
2. Select the Include inherited check box. Designer adds all inherited events to the list.
3. Locate and select the Execute_User event. 
4. Click Attach CLF. The Field Definition window appears.
5. Expand the UserFunctions node and select csi_SendEmail.
6. Click Select. Designer assigns the csi_SendEmail CLF to the Execute_User event and closes the 
Field Definition window. By attaching the logic here, the container logic (in this case a move 
standard) must complete before the e-mail is triggered.
7. Perform the "How to Set the User CLF Parameters" procedure.
How to Set the User CLF Parameters
Follow these steps to set the user CLF parameters to match the fields defined in the CDO:
1. Click the Logic Flows (CLFs) button. Designer displays the CLFs.
2. Select the csi_SendEmail CLF that you added earlier.
3. Click the CLF Parameters tab.
4. Click Add. Designer adds a generic parameter to the parameter list.
5. Repeat step 4 until you have five generic parameters.
6. Modify the properties for each parameter so that you have these parameters:
• Recipient 
• Sender
• Subject
• Message 
• EmailTransport

Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide 4-1
Chapter 4: Tracing Transactions
 
Introduction
Tracing ability in Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR facilitates development, debugging, and 
troubleshooting of Configurable Logic Flows (CLFs) by users who are not C++ developers. These topics 
explain how to enable the Transaction Tracing utility and how to read trace files. 
Transaction Tracing is a debugging tool provided with Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR for users who 
want to create advanced new or modified CLFs to extend system logic. 
When building a CLF in the Designer, if you supply an incorrect parameter to an execution, use an 
execution in an unadvised manner, or set up the CLF logic such that it does not work as desired in the 
context of the transaction and associated objects, isolating the problem is difficult without a debugging 
tool. 
Trace files are generated during the execution of transactions when Transaction Tracing is enabled. With 
the program information written out to the trace file, you can easily determine the cause of CLF problems.
The Tracing utility, csiTraceLogUtil, is installed with the Application Servers and is located in this folder:
C:\Program Files (x86)\Camstar\InSite Server
A folder called Transaction Logs is created to store trace files:
C:\Program Files (x86)\Camstar\Logs\Transaction Logs
In This Chapter
This chapter contains these topics:
• Features and Parameters
• Enabling Transaction Tracing
• Disabling Transaction Tracing
• Reading Trace Files
• Mapping With Designer
• Sample Trace File

=== PDF PAGE 164 ===
Chapter4:TracingTransactions
4-2 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
Features and Parameters
Tracing can be enabled for the entire cluster or for a single user. The first line in each log file includes the 
username.
You determine whether user passwords appear in the Trace log by enabling or disabling the Expose in the 
user-defined fields area option of the EnteredPassword field in Designer. The fields required to mask user 
passwords are located in the Designer CDO section for WIPMsg fields. Refer to the Opcenter Execution 
Medical Device and Diagnostics Designer User Guide or the Opcenter Execution Core Designer User Guide 
for  information.
You can type csiTraceLogUtil at a command prompt to see the Help window for the command.
Help Window
This example shows the help window for the csiTraceLogUtil command. The available parameters for the 
csiTraceLogUtil command are:
csiTraceLogUtil [action] -l[trace level] -s[max list size] -u[user name]


=== PDF PAGE 165 ===
Chapter4:TracingTransactions
csiTraceLogUtil Parameters
This table defines the parameters included in the csiTraceLogUtil.
Parameter Enable or Disable
Trace Level There are six trace levels:
1. Traces CLF names only
2. Traces CLF names and function names
3. Traces CLF names, function names, and parameters
4. Traces CLF names, function names, parameters, and resolved values
5. Traces CLF names, function names, parameters, resolved values, and 
XML requests & response
6. Traces CLF names, function names, parameters, resolved values, XML requests & 
response, and commit event SQL statements
Level 3 is the default level.
Max list size Option applies only if the level is set to 4 or higher. When resolved parameter values 
are traced, this option controls how many elements from the list type parameters 
should be traced. The number of elements traced is either the number of elements 
in the list or the value of this option, whichever is smaller.
This option applies to Parameter Lists of any type or (Query) Parameters or Resultset 
parameter types.
User name Allows you to trace transactions for a single user. The default is to capture traces for 
the user who is currently logged in.
Limitations
Tracing should only be used to troubleshoot problems. System performance is significantly impacted when 
tracing is enabled. However, running the  Application Server in a multi-user environment is possible even if 
tracing is turned on.
Resolved Parameter Values
Several parameters are traced when the trace level is set to 4 or higher, and the max list size is set to 
greater than 0. The trace messages have these formats:
1. List Parameters (Type Multiplicity)
The number of items traced is based on the Trace Level parameter in the Trace Log utility.
Text Format: ParameterName, resolved value = ( [List, size=5] 0005d2800000000e, 
0005d2800000000f, 0005d28000000010, ... )
2. Multi-Value Parameters (Multiplicity)
Traces all the values of the parameter for each value used by the function.
Text Format: ParameterName, resolved values [Multiple, size=5, index=4] = (InstanceId)
Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide 4-3

=== PDF PAGE 166 ===
Chapter4:TracingTransactions
4-4 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
3. Out/Return Parameters
Not traced.
4. Resultset Parameters
The number of items traced is based on the Trace Level parameter in the Trace Log utility.
Text Format: ParameterName, resolved value = ( [Resultset, rows=1, columns=1] 
{0005a08000000005} )
ParameterName, resolved value = ( [Resultset, rows=5, columns=2] {Spreader 1, false}, 
{Spreader1, false}, {Spreader2, false}, {Spreader3, false}, ... )
5. Query Parameters
Traces all the Name-Value pairs.
Text Format: ParameterName, resolved value = ( [Query Parameters, size=2] FromStepId = 
00074e8000000006, PathTypeId = 1440 )
6. Field Reference Parameters
The resolved value not traced. However, text indicating that it is a Field Reference is traced.
Text Format: ListField, resolved value = [Field Reference] )
Fields, resolved value = [Field Reference, Multiple, size=6] )

=== PDF PAGE 167 ===
Chapter4:TracingTransactions
Enabling Transaction Tracing
Enable csiTraceLogUtil when you execute transactions if you want  to generate a trace file. Make sure the 
Application Servers are running before you enable the utility.
How to Enable Transaction Tracing
Follow these steps to enable Transaction Tracing:
1. Click Start > Windows System > Command Prompt. The Command prompt window appears.
2. Change the directory to InSite Server under Program Files (x86)>Camstar 
(C:\Program Files (x86)\Camstar\InSite Server).
3. Type csiTraceLogUtil enable. A line is displayed stating that trace is enabled.
You can also enter parameters in this format:
csiTraceLogUtil enable -l[trace level] -s[max list size] -u[user name]
Do not include brackets [ ] in the command line. 
This example sets the trace level to 4, the max list size to 3, and the user to insiteadmin:
csitracelogutil enable -l4 -s3 -uInSiteAdmin
Note: The Transaction Tracing utility is case-sensitive and requires that you enter user 
names as they appear in the application.
4. Close the  Administrator: Command Prompt window.
Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide 4-5

=== PDF PAGE 168 ===
Chapter4:TracingTransactions
4-6 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
Disabling Transaction Tracing
Disabling tracing when not needed improves system performance. 
How to Disable Transaction Tracing
Follow these steps to disable Transaction Tracing:
1. Click Start > Windows System > Command Prompt. The Command prompt window appears.
2. Change the directory to InSite Server under Program Files (x86)>Camstar. 
(C:\Program Files (x86)\Camstar\InSite Server).
3. Enter csiTraceLogUtil disable at the next prompt. A line is displayed stating trace is disabled.
4. Close the command prompt. 

=== PDF PAGE 169 ===
Chapter4:TracingTransactions
Reading Trace Files
A separate trace file is generated for every transaction performed while tracing is enabled. Trace logs are 
stored in the Transaction Logs folder. Starting a new container or moving an existing container are two 
examples of transactions that are captured in a transaction log.
Trace File Location
The log files are stored in:
Program Files (x86)\Camstar\Logs\Transaction Logs
Sample Trace File Location
This example shows a sample Transaction log folder with a number of trace files.
Trace File Naming Convention
Files are named using this convention:
yyyymmdd_time_process id._thread id_username_ServiceTypeName.log
Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide 4-7

=== PDF PAGE 170 ===
Chapter4:TracingTransactions
4-8 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
The 20140619_201915639_8516_7616_CamstarAdmin_MoveIn.log file documents the transaction 
MoveIn done on June 19, 2014 at 15 seconds and 639 milliseconds after 8:19 pm with a process ID of 
8516 and a thread ID of 7616.
Sample Trace File
This example shows the beginning of a sample trace file. A larger portion of the file is shown in the topic, 
"Sample Trace File." 
Trace File Format
The log files have three columns.
Columns
The first column is the date and time, with the milliseconds separated by a dot. 
The second column is the thread ID.
The third column contains information on CDO and field events. For XML clients, the first line of the third 
column contains the name of the transaction and the user in this format:
starting transaction <transaction name> for <user name>
For example, in the example  the first line was:
starting transaction MoveIn for CamstarAdmin
where MoveIn is the transaction name and CamstarAdmin is the user name.

=== PDF PAGE 171 ===
Chapter4:TracingTransactions
The rest of the third column has these formats:
For field events:
Depth:<number > CDO: <cdo name> Field: <field name> Event: <event name>
Exec: <execution name>
 Value = <parameter value>
The <> indicates a variable. For example, the second line in the previous illustration is:
Depth: 1 CDO: CollectDataPoints Field: Constants Event: Initialize
where 1 is the depth number, CollectDataPoints Field is the transaction name, Constants is the field value, 
and Initialize is the event name.
For CDO events:
Depth:<number > CDO: <transaction name> Perform: <method>
Exec: <execution name>
Event = <parameter value>
The <> indicates a variable. For example, a CDO event from the illustration shown is:
Depth: 2 CDO: MoveIn Perform: ResolveDataCollectionDef
where 2 is the depth number, MoveIn is the CDO, and ResolveDataCollection Def is the method.
Depth
There can be a large number of nested calls, each one of them indented. This image shows these nesting 
levels  as a depth number for ease of reading and printing the trace files.
Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide 4-9

Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide 5-1
Chapter 5: Clustering Application 
Servers
 
Introduction
Clustering enables you to use multiple Application Servers to balance the load of transactions. Clustering 
improves transaction throughput and system availability and reliability. Siemens supports clustering using 
Network Load Balancing (NLB).
This section provides information on configuring Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR systems for NLB. 
Follow the guidelines to ensure a smooth implementation. 
In This Chapter
This chapter contains these topics:
• Before You Begin
• Clustering Topology
• Setting Up NLB Clusters
• Connections from the XML Client Application
Related Documentation
Refer to "Best Practices" for information on load balancing and high availability clustering. 

=== PDF PAGE 182 ===
Chapter5:ClusteringApplicationServers
5-2 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
Before You Begin
Consider clustering recommendations, NIC teaming, and prerequisites before you begin.
Recommended Reading
Consult the Microsoft clustering reference documentation for information on Network Load Balancing 
before proceeding. 
About NIC Teaming
Network Interface Card (NIC) teaming is the process of grouping several physical NICs into one single, 
logical NIC, which can be used for network fault tolerance. In theory, if one physical NIC fails, the network 
connection is not be lost, rather the traffic continues over the other NICs. 
Important:Using Redundant NICs (NIC Teaming) in either a cluster or standalone environment is 
not recommended or supported for Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR 
implementation servers including IIS,  WCF Services, the Application Server, and 
database servers. Do not configure hardware teaming on network adapters when using 
Microsoft's NLB.
Using NIC teaming In a clustered environment can cause the  Application Server cached data to be out of 
sync. 
Prerequisites
This topic provides system prerequisites to configure NLB clusters.
Each cluster node requires:
• Windows Server  2019. 
• Two network interface cards (NICs), a frontend and a backend, both enabled
• A static IP address on both NICs (NLB is not supported if your nodes are obtaining IP addresses 
through DHCP)
• Application Server
A cluster requires:
• One virtual IP address to be used by all cluster nodes
• A cluster name
• A fully qualified domain name
The Application Servers have specific prerequisites. Refer to the Release Matrix on Support Center.

=== PDF PAGE 183 ===
Chapter5:ClusteringApplicationServers
Clustering Topology
Siemens recommends the following topology for clusters.
Note the following about the topology:
• Each cluster node contains all of the listed configurations.
• Each cluster node contains two network cards (one for the front end and one for the back end).
• Each cluster node has its own static IP address.
• The first node added to a cluster becomes the cluster controller. The cluster controller uses a 
virtual IP address that is used as the cluster IP address.
• A cluster is created for product-related services.
Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide 5-3

=== PDF PAGE 184 ===
Chapter5:ClusteringApplicationServers
5-4 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
Setting Up NLB Clusters
The first cluster node is the Cluster Controller. In the course of adding cluster nodes, you will be entering a 
common IP cluster address to all nodes. If you get an error message saying there is a conflict because the 
IP address is in use, ignore it and close the message window. Once the cluster setup is completed for all 
nodes, the error message no longer appears.
Prerequisites
Verify these items are in place before proceeding:
• A virtual IP address to be entered during NLB configuration
• A static IP address unique to each node in the cluster
Task Overview
You must perform these tasks to set up Network Load Balancing:
1. Set up or obtain TCP/IP Parameters.
2. Set up network load balancing.
Task 1: Set Up or Obtain TCP/IP Parameters
Refer to the Microsoft website for information on setting up TCP/IP for Network Load Balancing.
Tips
• The IP address must be unique to this system and static.
• Make a note of the subnet mask value. You will be entering it for the NLB configuration.
Task 2: Set Up Network Load Balancing
Refer to the Microsoft website for information on configuring Network Load Balancing parameters.
Tips on the Cluster Parameters Window
• Enter the  cluster IP address that will be common to all nodes of this cluster in the IP Address field. 
This is a virtual IP address. Make a note of it because you will be entering it for every node of the 
cluster.
• Enter the  subnet mask value that you entered in Task 1 in the Subnet mask field.
• Enter the  preferred name for this cluster in a format similar to the following in the Full internet 
name field:
<ClusterName>.mydomain.local
Write down the full internet name—you will be entering it for every node of the cluster. You will 
also use the cluster name from your XML Client application.
• Click the Multicast option. 

=== PDF PAGE 185 ===
Chapter5:ClusteringApplicationServers
• If selected, clear the Allow remote control check box.
Tips on the Port Rules Window
• Port rules must be the same for all nodes of the cluster; otherwise, nodes will not be admitted for 
membership when they try to join.
• Configure the port rules as follows to maximize traffic handling:
a. Select any existing Port Rules configuration and click Remove. 
b. Verify that the All check box is selected for the Cluster IP address.
c. Enter the  range of port numbers in the cluster In the Port range fields. If you don’t know the 
exact range, keep the default values.
d. Select the option that matches your environment in Protocols. If you are not sure, select Both 
to ensure coverage.
e. Select Multiple host in  Filtering mode.
f. Select one of the following Affinity settings:
• None, if you want to support the Application Server cluster only 
• Single, if you want to support the Application Server cluster 
g. For Load weight, do one of the following:
• Enter a specific number denoting the percentage of traffic load this particular node will 
handle. For example, if you want this node to handle 50% of the load, type 50.
• Select Equal to distribute traffic evenly among nodes.
Tips on the Connect Window
• Enter the  name of a node that will be part of the new cluster in the Host field.
Tips on the Host Parameters Window
• Enter or select a unique number from 1 to the maximum number of nodes in the cluster in the 
Priority (unique host identifier) field.
• The number you enter specifies the node that takes over if one node fails. The lower the number, 
the higher the priority.
• Enter the  system’s static address in the IP address field. This must match the IP address entry in 
Task 2.
• Enter the  subnet mask in the Subnet mask field. This must match your entry in Tasks 1 and 2.
• Select Started in the Default state field to specify that the node join the cluster immediately when 
started.

Connections from the XML Client Application
The XML Client application must use the cluster name as host name to connect to the XML Server. These 
parameters are passed from XML Client applications to the XML Server:
port number = 443
host = the name of the cluster, as in mycluster
Note: The configuration of the MfgAuditTrail does not need to be updated to point to the cluster 
address. By default each MfgAuditTrail installation points to the local  Application Server  
which is the desired configuration. The configuration information above refers to non-
Opcenter EX MDD or non-Opcenter EX CR delivered XML clients.
As a network administrator, you must pass this information on to the developer who is writing programs 
using the Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR XML Client APIs. Refer to C:\Program Files 
(x86)\Camstar\InSite XML Client\docs\NETClientAPIspecDoc.chm for information.

Chapter 6: Application Server
 
Introduction
The Application Server runs in IIS as a dedicated application pool. The WWW service enables you to keep 
the Application Server running when the maximum memory is reached. The service automatically starts a 
new Application Server instance whenever the original Application Server instance reaches its maximum 
memory usage or experiences a crash.
In This Chapter
This chapter contains these topics:
• Configuring the Maximum Memory Limit for the Application Pool
• Monitoring Application  Server Performance
• Viewing Application  Server Events for Recycling

=== PDF PAGE 188 ===
Chapter6:ApplicationServer
6-2 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
Configuring the Maximum Memory Limit for the 
Application Pool
You can configure the maximum memory limit setting  on the application pool in IIS.
When Configuring the Maximum Memory Limit for the Application Pool
The default value for the maximum memory limit is 4194304 KB (4 GB). You can edit this setting using the 
Recycling or Advanced Settings option in IIS.
Consider your day-to-day operations when determining the size you need for the maximum memory limit. 
Some activities can push the setting past its threshold, causing the Application Server to stop and start a 
new Application Server. For example, Siemens recommends you increase the limit if you are using:
•  Change Management and activating packages with 1000 or more instances (including 
references), or the
• Export/Import utility to either export or import 1000 or more instances (including references).
Edit Application Pool Recycling Settings
This image shows an example of the Edit Application Pool Recycling Settings dialog box.


=== PDF PAGE 189 ===

This image shows an example of the Advanced Settings dialog box.
How to Configure the Maximum Memory Limit for the Application Pool
Follow these steps to configure the maximum memory limit for the application pool:
1. Click Start > Server Manager. The Server Manager window appears.
2. Select Internet Information Services (IIS) Manager from the Tools menu. The Internet 
Information Services (IIS) Manager window appears.
3. Select the server in the Connections pane. The Home window for the server appears.
4. Select Application Pools in the Connections pane. The Application Pools pane appears.
5. Select CamstarAppServer. The Actions pane appears.
6. Are you using the Recycling or Advanced Settings option?

Pool Recycling Settings dialog box 
appears.
b. Enter a value for Private Memory 
Usage (in KB) and click Next.
c. Click Finish. The Application Pools 
pane appears.
Advanced Settings a. Click Advanced Settings. The 
Advanced Settings dialog box appears.
b. Enter a value for Private Memory 
Limit (KB) and click OK. The 
Application Pools pane appears.

Monitoring Application  Server Performance
Performance counters are measurements of a system's rate or activity, which can be included in the 
operating system or be part of individual applications. Windows Performance Monitor requests the current 
value of performance counters at specified time intervals.
When Monitoring Application Server Performance
 IIS provides counters which you can add through the Add Counters dialog box in the Performance 
Monitor. Specifically, you can add the APP_POOL_WAS counter to monitor the number of recycles. You can 
add the W3SVC_W3WP counter to monitor transactions per second.

How to Add New Performance Counters
Follow these steps to add new performance counters:
1. Click Start > Windows Administrative Tools > Performance Monitor. The Performance Monitor 
window appears.
2. Expand Monitoring Tools.
3. Select Performance Monitor. The Performance Monitor appears.
4. Click Add. The Add Counters dialog box  appears.
5. Expand APP_POOL_WAS in the list of counters. 
6.  Select Total Application Pool Recycles to monitor the number of recycles.
7. Select CamstarAppServer in the Instances of selected object list.
8. Click Add to add the counter to the Added Counters list.
9. Expand W3SVC_W3WP in the list of counters. 
10.  Select Requests / Sec to monitor transactions per second.
11. Select <process ID>_CamstarAppServer in the Instances of selected object list.
12. Click Add to add the counter to the Added Counters list.
13. Click OK. The added counters appear in the Performance Monitor window.

Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide A-1
Appendix A: Database Identifiers
 
Introduction
This topic discusses database identifiers, including considerations and limitations. 
Definitions
These key terms are related to database identifiers.
Term Definition
OID Object Identifier (OID) is a synonym for instance identifier. It consists of a 24-bit 
Object Type Identifier (CDODefID) and a 40-bit Unique Identifier (UID). 
CDODefID A Configurable Data Object Definition Identifier (CDODefID) is automatically assigned 
to a class defined in the Designer application. It is part of the OID to determine an 
object's class. CDODefID is also referred to as object type identifier.
DBID The Database Identifier (DBID) is used to create unique instance identifiers across the 
enterprise. A DBID is part of the OID.
UID A Unique Identifier (UID) is assigned to an object to uniquely identify it within its 
object type. It is part of the OID.
In This Appendix
This appendix contains these topics:
• Database ID Overview
• DBID Assignment Considerations
• DBID Limitations
• DBID Tables

=== PDF PAGE 194 ===
AppendixA:DatabaseIdentifiers
A-2 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
Database ID Overview
An enterprise-wide factory can require running multiple instances of Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR 
and their components as a distributed system. An instance of Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR  is 
defined by the database; that is, an instance can be a standalone server or a clustered server.
In a factory environment, Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR  instances exchange data objects, invoke 
remote transactions on objects, and consolidate their production data in a central repository — a data 
warehouse. Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR ’s objects are assigned an identifier, independently 
generated at each Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR  instance. Identifiers must be unique not only at an 
instance level but across the enterprise.
For this purpose, Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR create a system-level identification for each 
Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR  instance. This identification is referred to as a database identifier 
(DBID).
Database Identifiers
A database identifier is a system-level designation of an Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR  instance 
served by a single database management system. It is associated with a site name that must be identical to 
the site name given in Management Studio. 
The configuration of the database identifier is required. The Application Server will not launch without it. 
The configuration of the database identifier includes settings that provide the system with information 
about the enterprise. These additional settings are referred to as global DBID settings.
Object Identifiers
Object identifiers are generated by the system whenever new objects are created. This diagram shows the 
implementation of object identifiers using the database identifier:
The CDODefID is of fixed length and has a size of 24 bits. The DBID and UID share a size of 40 bits, but 
each requires a minimum of 1 bit.

=== PDF PAGE 195 ===
AppendixA:DatabaseIdentifiers
Transaction Identifiers
Transaction identifiers are generated by the system whenever transactions are executed. This diagram 
shows the implementation of transaction identifiers using the database identifier:
The DBID and UID share a size of 63 bits, but each requires a minimum of one bit. The one bit at the start 
of the TXNID is reserved for Siemens internal purposes.
Padding Area
The implementation of object identifiers and transaction identifiers leaves a padding area between the 
DBID and the UID. This allows flexible growth of both the DBID and the UID towards each other, as follows:
• The UID starts with the lowest presentable value in ascending order. 
• The DBID starts with the lowest presentable value in ascending order, but the bit field is reversed 
and shifted to the left of the UID bit field.
Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide A-3

=== PDF PAGE 196 ===
AppendixA:DatabaseIdentifiers
A-4 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
DBID Assignment Considerations
The database identifier (DBID) must be unique for each site in the enterprise. What number to assign to 
what site must be decided from a central location or mutually agreed upon by managers at each site. For 
the actual assignment of the DBID, each site configures its own DBID using the mutually agreed upon 
numbering.
The system administrator must consider these questions before configuring an Opcenter EX MDD or 
Opcenter EX CR instance:
• How many sites will be part of the Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR  installation?
The number of sites will determine the range of identifiers reserved for the enterprise. You can 
assign an overestimated number to allow for future expansion. 
• What are the site names assigned to the Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR  instances?
The site name for each site must match the Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR  site name 
entered in Management Studio.
• Are you planning to consolidate your databases into a central repository?
If you have multiple Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR  sites that will use a centralized repos-
itory of information, note that the default logins, CamstarAdmin, Administrator, and InSiteAdmin, 
have a default DBID of 0 across sites. By that definition, the uniqueness is not preserved across 
sites. If your sites have been using these default logins, DBIDs will collide when you consolidate 
your data in the central repository. 
To prevent such collisions, the recommended process is:
1) After installing the Client, use one of the default logins to start Portal. 
2) Create your own employee definitions (logins).
3) Delete the default logins.
4) If you want to continue to use the default login names, add them back after deleting 
them. This ensures that your own additions will have unique DBIDs.

=== PDF PAGE 197 ===
AppendixA:DatabaseIdentifiers
DBID Limitations
These rules apply to DBIDs:
• Each site must use a database identifier that is unique in the enterprise. Assign database 
identifiers in sequential order for easy administration.
Note: The database identifier 0 (zero) is reserved for Siemens use.
• Do not reassign a database identifier that has been used in production, even if you remove the 
site in the future.
• Do not change the DBID for a site that is already in production.
Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide A-5

=== PDF PAGE 198 ===
AppendixA:DatabaseIdentifiers
A-6 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
DBID Tables
DBID information is stored in three tables:
• DBIDConfiguration
• DBIdentifier
• DBIdentifierHistory
The key symbol indicates the key column of the table.
Important:Do not make any changes to these tables unless requested by Product Support. 
DBIDConfiguration
This table contains general information on the enterprise in relation to the database identifier. It defines 
attributes that are used to monitor sites and to incorporate runtime safety mechanisms. This table must 
contain only one entry.
The DBIDConfiguration table contains these fields.
 Field Description
 EstimatedNumberOfSitesValue that is defined by the system administrator and provides 
Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR  with an estimated number 
of sites in the enterprise. This count is only an estimate and can 
be changed at any time, but it must be of higher value than the 
amount of sites defined in the DBIdentifier table.
NumberOfBitsUsed System-generated value that defines the number of bits required 
to represent the estimated number of sites.
WarningRange Number of identifiers available (out of the more than two 
thousand million possible) at which point the system 
administrator is notified that the system is running out of 
identifiers. Keep the default value
Ñ IDColumn Unique ID for the DBConfiguration table.
DBIdentifier Table
This table manages the assignments of database identifiers to sites. It may contain one entry for each site 
in the enterprise. The assignment of an identifier is a prerequisite to make the system operational. It is 
prohibited to assign the same identifier to multiple sites, and it is not recommended to reuse identifiers 
that have previously been assigned.

=== PDF PAGE 199 ===
AppendixA:DatabaseIdentifiers
The DBIdentifier table contains these fields.
Field Description
DBIdentifier Database identifier for a site in 10-character hex representation 
(hexadecimal). This identifier will become part of the transaction identifiers 
and object identifiers.
SiteName Name of the site to which the database identifier is assigned. This name 
must match the site name entered in Management Studio.
AssignmentTimestampSystem-generated date and time when the database identifier was assigned 
to the site.
DBIdentifierHistory Table
This history table is used to keep track of database identifiers that have previously been assigned to any 
site in the enterprise. When a site is removed, its entry is moved from the DBIdentifier table to the 
DBIdentifierHistory table and a removal timestamp is added to the history.
The DBIdentifierHistory table contains this information.
 Field Description
Ñ DBIdentifier Database identifier that had previously been assigned.

FAQs: Implementation
This topic covers possible implementation questions.
Number of Servers
Is there a way to determine the number of servers required for the number of users we will have at our 
site?
Answer
Yes. Siemens has a process to help you determine the correct hardware configuration. Refer to  "Server 
Configuration Requirements" and "Server Sizing Information" in the Installation Guide.  
Clustered vs Single Server
Is it better to run a clustered environment or a single server environment?
Answer
Siemens recommends cluster sizes of up to 10 nodes.
Siemens also recommends load balancing for the  Application Server. This enables you to apply Service 
Packs and upgrade to new releases in a controlled manner. 
Siemens recommends hardware load balancing for environments requiring transaction volumes of 50 
transactions per second and above. Refer to “Load Balancing”  for information.
Security Implementation
How will we implement security in our new Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR implementation?
Answer
Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR use role-based access control as its security model. This security 
model enables complex role-based authorizations while simplifying permission management.  Refer to the 
Security Guide for information on the role-based access control model used by Opcenter EX MDD or 
Opcenter EX CR.
License Management for Additional Site
We have a current  license management configuration in place. Do we need to perform license 
management procedures if we are installing a new site? 
Answer
Refer to  the Opcenter Execution Medical Device and Diagnostics Installation Guide or the Opcenter 
Execution Core Installation Guide for information on how licensing works.

AppendixB:BestPractices
Installation Best Practices
These are some best practice guidelines to consider before installation:
• Install Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR on a clean server with Windows Server 2019  before 
any custom local security polices or third-party product installation.
• Install on a Windows server with a RAID1 or RAID5 disk.
• Verify the Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR installation before installing third-party products, 
including antivirus software.
• Install the latest set of Windows security patches.
• Make sure you are using an account that has Administrative privileges on the server and is also a 
user on the domain. You must always login to the domain, not the local computer.
• Do not configure hardware teaming on network adapters.
• Develop naming conventions, if not already in place, for server names, Windows service account 
names, and Oracle database instance and schema names. Pay attention to naming conflicts as 
they relate to multi-site test and production systems.
• Use Source Control software such as Microsoft's  Team Foundation Server or PVCS to manage 
changes to the InSite.mdb file,  and all code or files that are, or will be, modified as part of your 
Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR implementation.
Redundant NICs (NIC Teaming) in a Cluster
NIC teaming is the process of grouping several physical NICs into one single logical NIC that, along with 
other things, can be used for network fault tolerance.  So, if one physical NIC fails, in theory the network 
connection would not be lost. Instead the traffic would continue over the other NICs.  
This in itself is not a problem. However, in a clustered environment using either F5 BIG-IP or NLB, it can be 
a problem because:
• F5 BIG-IP - “Redundant NICs implement failover by using one shared IP address for two NICs. 
When the BIG-IP Controller load balances traffic, it tracks the last hop MAC address in order to 
send return traffic to the same MAC address. As a result, BIG-IP may continue to send traffic to the 
inactive NIC after a failover occurs between the two NICs."
• NLB - "NLB uses a shared virtual media access control address. The shared virtual media access 
control address is used instead of the physical adapter's media access control address for all 
communications to the load-balanced IP addresses. With some teaming adapters, the virtual 
media access control address cannot  overwrite the physical media access control address. This 
causes an IP address conflicts in the cluster."
Note: Using Redundant NICs (NIC Teaming) in a cluster environment is not recommended or 
supported at this time for Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR implementation servers 
including IIS), Application Server, and database servers. 
Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide B-5

=== PDF PAGE 206 ===
AppendixB:BestPractices
B-6 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
Post-Installation Tips
• Set event logs to wrap and increase the size, if necessary. Otherwise you can lose the most recent 
errors.
• Verify any applications that write to logs. You should also have a procedure for clearing those logs 
to avoid filling them (such as SPC's graphic image files).
• Set the Recovery Model to Simple for any MS SQL Server databases for which transaction logs are 
not saved. This applies to both a development and a test environment.
• Set Transaction Logs for SQL Server to back up at least once per day.
• Disable Compression on all operations for  Oracle tablespaces. Siemens does not support schema, 
tablespace, or table compression. 
• RECYCLEBIN must be disabled for Oracle databases.

AppendixB:BestPractices
FAQs: Installation
This topic explains how to resolve possible installation issues.
HTTP 404 - File Not Found Error
Why do I receive the “HTTP 404 - file not found error” after I installed Opcenter EX MDD (or Opcenter EX 
CR) on a Windows Server 2019 machine?
Answer
It is very likely you did not install ASP.NET following the Opcenter EX MDD (or Opcenter EX CR) installation. 
You also need to enable the ASP.NET Web Service Extension in IIS 10. The procedures that follow are for 
Windows Server 2019.
How to Install ASP.NET on Windows Server 2019
Follow these steps to install ASP.NET on Windows Server 2019:
1. Click Start > Server Manager. The Server Manager window appears.
2. Select Add Roles and Features from the Manage menu. The Add Roles and Features Wizard 
appears.
3. Click Next three times to advance to  Server Roles.
4. Navigate to Web Server (IIS) > Web Server > Application Development in the tree.
5. Click the ASP.NET 4.7 and ISAPI Extensions check boxes, and then click the Next button. A 
confirmation dialog box appears. 
6. Click Install. The ASP.NET 4.7 and ISAPI Extensions are installed. 
7. Click Finish.
How to Enable the ASP.Net Web Service Extension in IIS 10 on Windows Server 2019:
Follow these steps to enable the ASP.NET Web Service Extensions in IIS 10 on Windows Server 2019:
1. Click Start > Windows Administrative Tools > Computer Management. The Computer 
Management window appears.
2. Expand Services and Applications and then click Internet Information Services (IIS) Manager. 
The Connections pane appears.
3. Click the server node in the Connections pane. The Camstar Server Home pane appears. 
4. Double-click ISAPI and CGI Restrictions under IIS. The ISAPI and CGI Restrictions pane appears. 
5. Click ASP.NET  v4.0.30319  and then click Edit in the Actions pane. The Edit ISAPI and CGI 
Restrictions dialog box appears.
6. Click the Allow extension path to execute check box if not already selected, and then click OK. 
7. Repeat steps 5 - 6 for the remaining entries in the ISAPI and CGI Restrictions list.
8. Close the IIS Manager window.

Optimizing Performance and Scaling High Transaction 
Volume
You can optimize Microsoft SQL Server performance and scale database operations to improve efficiency. 
Important: If you plan to enable Microsoft SQL Server Read Committed Snapshot Isolation (RCSI), 
Siemens recommends consulting with database experts to thoroughly test and evaluate 
the impact of enabling this feature on your specific database.
Optimizing Microsoft SQL Server Performance
You can change the default settings for several SQL Server options to improve performance. This table 
shows the default settings and recommended settings for these options.
Object Option Default SettingRecommended Setting

Max degree of parallelism
Max degree of parallelism determines the maximum number of processor cores that can be used by a 
single query execution. It is set to 0 by default indicating that SQL Server can choose a degree of 
parallelism as high as the number of logical processor cores. Set this option to a number no higher than 
the number of cores in a single non-uniform memory access (NUMA) node.
This example sets the max degree of parallelism option to 8:
sp_configure 'show advanced options', 1;
GO
RECONFIGURE WITH OVERRIDE;
GO
sp_configure 'max degree of parallelism', 8;
GO
RECONFIGURE WITH OVERRIDE;
GO

=== PDF PAGE 209 ===
AppendixB:BestPractices
Number of Data Files and Autogrowth Scheme
Mild tempdb contention can occur if the number of data files is set to the default setting of one. Adding 
one data file for each CPU core on the system and changing the Autogrowth scheme to a percentage 
instead of a static value addresses the issue.
You can set these options in Microsoft SQL Server Management Studio and must have system 
administrator permissions. The default user is "sa."
Refer to this Microsoft website for information on optimizing tempdb performance:
• https://technet.microsoft.com/en-us/library/ms175527(v=sql.105).aspx
Optimize for Adhoc Workloads
If SELECT queries produce a high number of single-use plans, you can improve the plan matching process 
by setting the Optimize for Adhoc Workloads option.
You can set these options in Microsoft SQL Server Management Studio and must have system 
administrator permissions. The default user is "sa."
Refer to these Microsoft websites for information on server configuration:
• https://msdn.microsoft.com/en-us/library/ms189631(v=sql.120).aspx
• https://msdn.microsoft.com/en-us/library/cc645587(v=sql.120).aspx
You can use the Opcenter Dynamic Purge Tool (DPT) to purge inactive  data from a transaction (OLTP) 
database by using the option to archive into Archive Database. Taking this action will enhance database 
performance. DPT enables you to purge data from transaction databases  while optionally archiving it to a 
designated archive database. The tool also provides the ability to restore archived data through the 
available restoration methods. DPT is currently designed for use with SQL Server database schemas only. 
Refer to the Opcenter Dynamic Purge document for information on how to setup, configure, and use DPT.
Employing Scaling and Improving Performance
Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR can be:
•  Scaled up by increasing the system resources on the servers (additional processors, memory)
• Scaled out by deploying one or more servers in one or more clusters to meet increasing demand
The key to improving performance is to identify the bottlenecks and address them with the following:
• Hardware changes
• Software configurations

Recommended Server Settings
Siemens recommends making these changes to your servers to improve response time:
• Enable temporary client-caching of static files (for example, images, scripts, styles, and so on) to 
reduce the amount of HTTP/S requests/responses between the browser and the Application 
Server. This is achieved by setting, within IIS, the HTTP/S response header to expire the web 
content within a specific period of time (for example, 1 day or more).
• Ensure the debug mode has been disabled for the Portal runtime. This is achieved by setting, 
within the Portal configuration file, the Debug attribute to “false.” Debug mode is needed only 
when troubleshooting development issues.

B-12 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
Database Case Sensitivity in Opcenter EX MDD and 
Opcenter EX CR
Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR support case sensitivity. However, the databases these products 
support handle case sensitivity differently by default: 
• Oracle is case sensitive
• Microsoft SQL Server is not case sensitive
Case sensitivity is important when you name named data objects (NDOs), revisioned objects (ROs) 
(including the revision) and named subentity objects within Designer. The difference between a database 
that is  case sensitive and one that is case insensitive follows:
• Case Sensitivity: Case-sensitive databases do consider the case in an instance name. For example:
"CuttingPrewash" and "cuttingprewash" are considered unique names and therefore would not 
cause an error in Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR.
If you saved both of these instances to the database, they would be considered different objects 
that can be instantiated.
• Case Insensitivity: Case insensitive databases do not consider the case in an instance name. For 
example:
"CuttingPrewash" and "cuttingprewash" are considered the same name and would therefore 
cause an error in Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR because names must be unique.
How to Verify Case Sensitivity
Follow these steps to verify case sensitivity:
1. Click Start >  Opcenter Execution Core > Management Studio. The Management Studio 
window appears.
2. Expand the Sites node.
3. Expand the <sitename> node.
4. Expand the Configuration folder and click Configuration. The Configuration for <site> options 
appear.
5. Is the All case-sensitive instance names check box selected?
If  . . . Then . . .
Yes Go to step 12.
No Follow these steps:
a. Select the All case-sensitive instance names check 
box. 
b. Go to step 6. 
6. Click Save. The application displays a message indicating that you must apply the metadata 
changes to the transaction database.
7. Expand the Databases folder.

=== PDF PAGE 213 ===
AppendixB:BestPractices
8. Right-click the Transaction database and select Update Database. The Update Database dialog 
appears.
9. Select Create Indices.
10. Clear the Update Metadata and Schema and Create Stored Procedures check boxes. 
11. Click OK. The application applies the changes, closes the Update Database dialog, and displays a 
message that the update was successful.
12. Select Exit from the File menu to close Management Studio.

B-14 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
Linguistic Sorting in Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX 
CR
Object and instance lists are sorted alphabetically in the application. However, the databases Opcenter EX 
MDD and Opcenter EX CR support handle sorting characters with accent marks differently by default: 
• Microsoft SQL Server uses an accent-insensitive sorting method.
• Oracle uses an accent-sensitive (BINARY) sorting method.
Linguistic Sorting in Oracle Databases
Lists are sorted in Oracle databases by default using BINARY.  With BINARY, items are sorted based on the 
characters  encoding scheme, with accented characters appearing further down the sort than unaccented 
characters. For example, "Abc, Zab, Äbc".
Alternatively, lists may be sorted in Oracle databases using BINARY_AI. With BINARY_AI, items are sorted 
by an accent-insensitive  binary sort.  For example, "Abc, Äbc, Zab".
You can set alternate sorting methods by modifying the NLS_SORT Oracle database parameter. Refer to 
"Oracle Configuration" for information.
This table defines object and instance lists that rely on the database sorting method. Use of an accent-
sensitive sorting method can result in object and instance lists being displayed differently across the 
application in languages other than English. 
 Page Control 
Attach Documents Page Contains  Object picklist.
Manage Attachments Page Contains  Object picklist.
Package Search Page Contains Modeling Object picklist.
Package Search (Multiple) Page Contains Modeling Object picklist.
Content Change History Page Contains  Object picklist.
Modeling Contains Modeling Object list.
Master Data Catalog Page Contains  Object  grid.
Modeling Audit Trail Page Contains CDO Type picklist.

=== PDF PAGE 215 ===
AppendixB:BestPractices
After Restoring a Database Backup
When you restore a database from a database backup, there may be residual entries in the 
MSMQManagers table. Leaving these entries in the table will negatively affect your implementation.  Two 
problems will occur:
• Performance degradation
• Suspension of messages in the wrong environment
Therefore, you should always delete all entries in the table after a restore and before you begin using the 
transaction database.
How to Delete Leftover Entries in the MSMQManagers Table
Follow these steps to delete leftover entries in the MSMQManagers table:
1. Stop the services.
2. Truncate the MSMQmanagers table (remove all rows, but do not drop the table).
3. Restart the Application Server and related services. The MSMQmanagers table is repopulated 
based on the configuration for this installation.
How to Reset the TxnIDs and InstanceIDs
The transaction IDs may be out of sync after a database restoration if you attempt to execute a transaction 
and receive the "failed to execute SQL statement" message. This sometimes occurs following a database 
restoration.
Open your preferred tool for executing statements against the database and run the following stored 
procedure to fix this:
exec csiResetTxnID;
The Transaction IDs should now be in sync with the history in the database and you should be able to 
continue working.
This stored procedure is included with Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR. 
Contact Product Support if InstanceIDs are off due to a restore or other manual alteration, and you need 
help correcting them.

SQL Server-Specific Issues
These topics cover issues specific to Microsoft SQL Server databases:
• Connecting to Named Instances of SQL Server
• Enabling Log-in in a Non-Connected Network Environment
Connecting to Named Instances of SQL Server
When connecting to a named instance of SQL Server from Management Studio, SQL Server Browser must 
be started on that instance of SQL Server. DBReset works when targeting a named instance of SQL Server 
only if SQL Server Browser is running.
Note: You can configure the named instance with a static or dynamic port.
Enabling Log-in in a Non-Connected Network Environment
In a non-connected network environment where the network adapter is disabled, you must install the 
Microsoft Loopback adapter to be able to log in to the  Portal (for example, a training environment where 
the training machine hosts both the Application Server and the transaction database and the machine has 
no network connection).  Refer to the Microsoft Support site http://support.microsoft.com for information 
on installing the Loopback adapter.

AppendixB:BestPractices
Using SQL Server Availability Groups for High Availability 
and Disaster Recovery
SQL Server   AlwaysOn Availability Groups provide a high availability and disaster recovery (HADR) solution 
for Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR. AlwaysOn Availability Groups use Windows Server Failover 
Clustering (WSFC). You can use this solution in conjunction with a failover clustering instance (FCI), 
including AlwaysOn Failover Clustering. This configuration enables the Application Server to fail over if 
there are connection issues to the database. All connections to the database are cleared and a new 
connection is established.
Considerations and Configuration
AlwaysOn Availability Groups enable you to protect your server against unplanned and planned downtime. 
You can replicate multiple databases to multiple replica systems using synchronous or asynchronous 
replication of the transaction log.
Refer to https://msdn.microsoft.com/en-us/library/hh510260.aspx for information on and examples of 
setting up an HADR solution using AlwaysOn Availability Groups.
Sample Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR Configuration
This table shows a sample configuration that uses Microsoft’s HADR solution. It uses SQL Server  FCI for 
local high availability (HA) and AlwaysOn Availability Groups for disaster recovery (DR).

AppendixB:BestPractices
Oracle and Function-based Indexes
If your implementation uses an Oracle database, then you may want to define an additional Index 
(function-based index) on the tables that support your Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR objects in 
order to avoid full table scans during processing. These indexes may also be utilized by queries and reports 
if you are using the Upper function in conjunction with the Name columns. The indexes cannot be created 
by Designer.
If you use a function on the index column like "UPPER(lastname) = 'JONES'", the optimizer would not 
choose the index created on the lastname column.
Function-based indexes provide an efficient way to evaluate predicates involving functions as in the 
example above. With a function-based index, the optimizer can use an index range scan for queries with 
expressions in the WHERE clause.
Syntax Examples
Here are some examples that show correct syntax:
Create UNIQUE INDEX EmployeeName_Upr ON Employee 
(UPPER(EmployeeName));
CREATE INDEX expression_ndx 
ON mytable ((mycola + mycolc) * mycolb); 
CREATE INDEX example_ndx 
ON myexample (mycola, UPPER(mycolb), mycolc); 
Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide B-19

Oracle Database Administration
The administration of Oracle databases covers a wide range of activities and requires that the Database 
Administrator (DBA) be familiar with a number of areas including, but not limited to, database 
administration, operating system (OS) administration, and network administration. The DBA must know 
how to use available utilities to troubleshoot issues encountered by database users and isolate them to the 
system component that is experiencing problems.
The three main areas of responsibility for Oracle DBAs are: 
• Installation and configuration of the database
• Network management
• Database management
