Oracle Configuration
This topic provides guidelines to help configure the Oracle database.
Common Database Setup/Configuration Considerations
It is possible to encounter a situation where the setting for OPEN_CURSORS was not set high enough for 
the system to function. This can cause the Application Server to stop processing completely or appear to 
have a very large memory leak. We are recommending a minimum setting for OPEN_CURSORS of 10000. 
There is no performance overhead if the value is set higher, so if you already have or would like to set the 
value to something greater than 1000, you can do so without causing any issues.
Sample Database Creation Parameters
Use the following database settings when the database instance is initially created.
The AL32UTF8 character set and AL16UTF16 national character set enable the database to store multiple 
language characters other than English.
Verify that all your Oracle instances are using the same character set definitions.

=== PDF PAGE 223 ===
AppendixB:BestPractices
Oracle 19c Real Application Cluster (RAC) Database 
Considerations
Oracle RAC databases provide a “Services” feature to distribute workloads by routing work to the least 
loaded instances as well as instances that can handle additional load. Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX 
CR support database failover on RAC databases.
Configuration
You can configure Oracle RAC database cluster nodes  in two modes:
• Active-Active
• All nodes in the cluster are always available for the clients to connect to the database.
• In case of a failover, the failed (dead) nodes failover to any available running (live) node.
• Active-Passive
• Only a few nodes in the cluster are always available for the clients to connect to the 
database.
• Any remaining nodes are available for failover in case of an “active” node failure.
Refer to these topics on Oracle's website for information: 
• https://docs.oracle.com/en/database/oracle/oracle-database/19/racad/introduction-to-oracle-
rac.html#GUID-D04AA2A7-2E68-4C5C-BD6E-36C62427B98E
• https://docs.oracle.com/en/database/oracle/oracle-database/19/racad/index.html
• https://www.oracle.com/webfolder/technetwork/tutorials/architecture-diagrams/high-availability-
overview/docs/en/high-availability-overview.html
Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide B-23

=== PDF PAGE 224 ===

Considerations
Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR support database failover on Oracle 19c RAC databases as follows:
• RAC database failover is automatically detected by  Application Servers.
• All existing Application Server connections are disconnected and reconnected during a RAC 
database failover scenario.
• All inflight transactions  must be resubmitted.
Oracle 19c RAC Service Configuration
Create an Oracle RAC service for Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR applications using the following:
• Transparent Application Failover (TAF) Policy - Basic
• Connection Load Balancing (CLB) Goal -  Long
• Load Balancing Advisory - Service Time
• Fast Application Notification (FAN) for OCI and ODP.NET Applications

Modeling Import/Export Considerations
It is important to understand the following considerations when implementing the Modeling 
Export/Import function for Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR:
• Export/Import is not a replacement for a backup strategy.
• Export/Import is not  guaranteed to work across versions of Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR.
• Export/Import is not intended to be used as a master data migration mechanism, but may be used 
for small data volumes (import data from ERP).
• Export/Import is not intended to be used as a mechanism to promote entire factory models, but 
may be used for small data volumes of incremental changes. Large data volumes may adversely 
impact Application Server performance.
• Export/Import assumes the same database schemata between the systems. You should have 
applied the metadata to both databases. You can apply metadata changes using Management 
Studio
• Export/Import cannot be run while metadata changes are being applied to a database.
• The size of the import file is limited to  30 MB by default. If your export file is larger than this, you 
can increase the value for MaxAttachmentSizeLimit in the Portal Settings in Portal Studio. 
• Review the Private Memory Usage application pool setting   for the CamsarAppServer application 
pool.  You may need to increase the value when either exporting or importing 1000 or more 
instances (including references). Refer to "Configuring the Maximum Memory Limit for the 
Application Pool" for information on how to change the setting.
Refer to the Opcenter Execution Medical Device and Diagnostics Modeling User Guide or the Opcenter 
Execution Core Modeling User Guide for  information on the Modeling Export/Import function.

=== PDF PAGE 227 ===
AppendixB:BestPractices
Assigning GUIDs to Export/Import Keys with Null Values 
The AssignGUIDtoNullExportImportKeys.bat script finds null values in the ExportImportKey column and  
generates a random globally unique identifier (GUID) to assign to those keys. Assigning GUIDs to 
ExportImportKeys prevents modeling object subentities from being overwritten during the export import 
process.
You should run a dbupdate before running the AssignGUIDtoNullExportImportKeys.bat. Running the 
AssignGUIDtoNullExportImportKeys.bat populates any null values that still remain for ExportImportKey 
after a dbupdate.
When Running the Batch File
You must run the batch file from the Command prompt. Siemens recommends running this script as an 
administrator to ensure the script has access to the appropriate folders to write log files and read script 
files. The batch file is located here if you installed Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR in the default 
location:
C:\Program Files (x86)\Camstar\InSite Administration\Scripts
Important:You must install a SQL command line interface for SQL Server or Oracle. You must install 
SQL*Plus for Oracle environments. You must install sqlcmd for SQL Server 
environments. The batch scripts use SQL*Plus or sqlcmd to connect to the RDBMS to 
execute SQL scripts.
A comment section at the top of the script provides information on the parameters needed to pass in for 
an Oracle database and a SQL Server database.
The batch process creates a log file containing the following information: 
• Number of rows updated in the database
• List of tables updated in the database
• A success or failure message
The log file is saved in the C:\Program Files (x86)\Camstar\InSite Administration\Scripts folder.
All GUIDs should be assigned if the batch file runs successfully. You can re-run the batch file and the 
associated log file should show 0 rows updated. (All ExportImportKey values should have a generated 
value after the successful completion of the first execution.)
When Running the Batch File Against an Oracle Database
The batch file requires four parameters when executing against an Oracle database: 
• Database vendor, specifically ORACLE or oracle
• Oracle instance name for an Oracle non-container instance for database name for Oracle 
container instance
• Insite Database User Login (schema) 
• Insite Database User Login Password (schema password)
Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide B-27

B-28 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
Example:
C:\Program Files (x86)\Camstar\InSite Administration\Scripts> AssignGUIDtoNullExportImportKeys.bat 
oracle <instance name> <user/schema> <password>
The AssignGUIDtoNullExportImportKeys.bat executes sqlplus and invokes C:\Program Files 
(x86)\Camstar\InSite Administration\Scripts\Oracle\AssignGUIDtoNullExportImportKey.or.sql.
When Running the Batch File Against a SQL Server Database
The batch file requires five parameters when executing against a SQL Server database:
• Database vendor, specifically SQLSERVER or sqlserver 
• IP address of the SQL Server instance
• SQL Server database name
• Insite Database User Login (SQL Server login)
• Insite Database User Login Password (SQL Server login password) 
Example:
C:\Program Files (x86)\Camstar\InSite Administration\Scripts> AssignGUIDtoNullExportImportKeys.bat 
sqlserver <IP address> <database name> <user/schema> <password>
The AssignGUIDtoNullExportImportKeys.bat executes sqlcmd and invokes C:\Program Files 
(x86)\Camstar\InSite Administration\Scripts\Sql Server\AssignGUIDtoNullExportImportKey.sql.
How to Run the AssignGUIDtoNullExportImportKeys.bat File
Follow these steps to run the AssignGUIDtoNullExportImportKeys.bat file:
1. Click Start > Windows System and then right-click Command Prompt and select More > Run as 
administrator. The Administrator: Command prompt window appears.
2. Change the directory to C:\Program Files (x86)\Camstar\InSite Administration\Scripts.
3. Enter AssignGUIDtoNullExportImportKeys.bat and provide the necessary parameters for the 
database type.
4. Execute the script. The batch file updates the ImportExportkeys, displays the results, and creates 
a log file containing the results.
5. Close the Administrator: Command prompt window.

AppendixB:BestPractices
FAQs: Databases
This topic explains how to resolve and recover from database errors.
Database Restoration Error
When I restore from the test database to the production database, I receive the following error:
DataDirect ODBC Oracle Driver, Oracle ORA-00001: unique constraint (insite.transrevoids605) violated.
What does this error mean?
Answer
You need to update the TransactionIDTable to reflect the new Maximum transactionid. It may have gotten 
out of sequence  during the restore of the database.
Running the csiResetTxnId stored procedure will resolve this problem. CsiResetTxnId looks through the 
database for the last ID used, then increments that by 1. 
Refer to the procedure “How to Reset the TxnIDs and InstanceIDs.”
Database Error
I keep receiving an error message stating “database error: unique constraint (transrevoids) violated.” How 
can I fix this error?
Answer
This is another problem that can be resolved by running the csiResetTXNID stored procedure. Refer to the 
procedure in the above question to run csiResetTXNID.
Restore Error
I performed a Restore. Why do I receive a “failed to execute SQL statement” now when I attempt to run a 
transaction?
Answer
It is possible the transaction IDs may be out of sync. This sometimes happens with a restore of the 
database. 
This is another problem that can be resolved by running the csiResetTXNID stored procedure. Refer to the 
procedure in the above question to run csiResetTXNID.
After you run csiResetTXNID, the TransastionIDs should be in sync with the history in the database and you 
should be able to continue work.
Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide B-29

=== PDF PAGE 230 ===
AppendixB:BestPractices
B-30 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
Lost Connection Error
I receive an error on the Application Server or Web server saying I lost my connection to the database. Is 
this a problem with the Application Server or did the database fail?
Answer
Determine whether you can connect from the  Application Server to the database server. Follow these 
steps:
1. Download the DebugView tool from live.sysinternals.com.
2. Run the DbgView.exe file.
3. Verify the Capture Win32 and Capture Global Win32 options are both checked in the Capture 
menu. Product-related services output does appear in DebugView unless Capture Global Win32 is 
selected because product-related services run under Local System.
4. Check Clock Time in the View menu to force the output to include clock time in the Time column.
5. Click the magnifying glass toolbar button to start or stop the capture. The capture is stopped 
when that button has a red x superimposed on it.
6. Start the Application Server in the Services pane. Refer to "Managing Services" for information.
7. View any messages in the main DebugView window for errors. 
8. Check whether any error messages are displayed. DebugView  captures the traffic between you 
and Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR with regard to the database. 
9. Ping the network to determine if the database server is responding.
10. Does the database respond?
• If yes, test using client connectivity tools to connect to the database and issue a simple 
query. (The tool varies per database, such as PS/SQL with Oracle and Query Analyzer for SQL 
Server.) If it responds, Product Support needs to look for exceptions.
• If no, the local database administrator or server team needs to look at the database server.
Services Do Not Start
My services do not start after Network Load Balancing (NLB) has been configured. How do I start the 
services?
Answer
Check the Advanced Settings for Network Connections and verify the network adapter designated as the 
front-end adapter is first in the order.

=== PDF PAGE 231 ===
AppendixB:BestPractices
Backend NIC Configuration
We are having problems with our Backend Network Interface Card (NIC) configuration. What do I need to 
do to configure it?
Answer
Two network adapters are required for each server. Each network adapter must be on a different subnet. 
You can refer to one NIC as a Frontend adapter and the other as the Backend adapter. 
• The Backend adapter will be configured to enable communication between servers. 
• The Frontend adapter will be used to register the server to domain name system (DNS).
Refer to “Clustering Application Servers” for information.
Database Restoration Problem
I am having trouble restoring a SQL Server database with a different user account. Do I need to use the 
same user account?
Answer
The resolution for this issue is database specific and requires you follow the database vendor guidelines. 
Refer to this Microsoft website for information:
• Copy Databases with Backup and Restore
Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide B-31

AppendixB:BestPractices
B-32 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
FAQs: Oracle Databases
This topic covers the issues specific to Oracle databases. 
Number of Connections Needed
How many connections should be configured within Oracle to support Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX 
CR?
Answer
(Number of Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR transactions as defined below) * (number of threads 
cast; maximum is 12; average is 3) * (Number of Application Servers)
The Application Server creates a new database connection for each of these items:
• One for admin/metadata (per server)
• One for user login authentication (per worker thread)
• One per service (per worker thread)
• One per compound transaction (per worker thread)
• One for a few other tasks such as MSMQManagers (per server)
Database Open Connections
Why does Siemens always leave the connections open to the Oracle database, even for transactions that 
are rarely used?
Answer
Oracle is inefficient in the cleanup of connections (when you constantly connect and disconnect from the 
database). Siemens uses connection pooling to maximize transactional performance and overcome this 
Oracle connection cleanup issue.
ORA-12638 Message
What does an ORA-12638: Credential retrieval failed message mean?
Answer
The ORA-12638: Credential retrieval failed message is frequently related to the SQLNET.ORA setting 
SQLNET.AUTHENTICATION_SERVICES. To resolve this error, change the following line in ORA_
HOME\network\admin\sqlnet.ora:
SQLNET.AUTHENTICATION_SERVICES= (NTS) 
into this 
SQLNET.AUTHENTICATION_SERVICES=(NONE) 
This disables the OS-based authentication.

=== PDF PAGE 233 ===
AppendixB:BestPractices
Advanced Compression
Oracle 19c  provides an Advanced Compression feature that offers considerable storage savings. Can this 
feature be implemented with Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR?
Answer
Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR support tablespace compression only. Compression of other objects 
(for example, tables) is not supported by Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR.

AppendixB:BestPractices
B-34 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
Recommendations for a High Availability Infrastructure
This topic provides recommendations to establish a high availability infrastructure, reducing planned and 
unplanned system downtime. System administrators and operations managers play an equally important 
role in supporting manufacturing execution twenty-four/seven. 
High availability infrastructure design depends on close cooperation between solution architects and 
system administrators. System configuration and validation adapting to an ever-changing business 
environment requires forward planning by operations managers. 
This topic lists various solutions to establish a high availability infrastructure for Opcenter EX MDD and 
Opcenter EX CR. Approaches are not mutually exclusive, but may be combined to achieve a highly 
available solution with disaster recovery options, preventive maintenance capabilities, and well-defined 
upgrade and migration procedures.
Architectural solutions are also described, focusing on both software and hardware components to 
improve system availability, and administration procedures for prevention and minimization of downtime. 
Your hardware platform, operating system, and database management system vendors are good resources 
for additional information on maintenance and upgrade procedures.
The extent of solution implementation depends on the business requirements, the business impact in case 
of system downtime, and resources available. The evaluation of these factors is the first step you and your 
team have to take.
These topics cover issues specific to a high availability infrastructure:
• Causes of Downtime
• System Monitoring
• System Maintenance
• Load Balancing
• High Availability Clustering
• Log Shipping
• Replication

=== PDF PAGE 235 ===
AppendixB:BestPractices
Causes of Downtime
The first step to improving system availability is to identify the causes of downtime. A categorization into 
planned and unplanned downtime is an important distinction to establish a resilient infrastructure and 
procedures.
Planned downtime is often the result of system maintenance and configuration changes. Unplanned 
downtime can be traced back to hardware failures and software failures. Examples of hardware failures 
are defective memory module and hard disk drive failure, and examples of software failures are server 
component failure, malformed data input, malicious code like viruses and worms, among others.
The second step to improving system availability is the identification of the highest points of contention, 
enabling business to target the most likely causes for downtime.
Finally, a solid implementation of strategic solution designs with ongoing focus on preventive 
maintenance is crucial to stay within business constraints for time, budget, and resources.
System Maintenance
System maintenance is the most common cause for downtime. Hardware upgrades, operating system 
patches and server software replacements are examples for this type of planned outage. The definition of 
maintenance procedures ahead of time is crucial to minimize downtime.
For preventive maintenance, systems monitoring is required at both the hardware and software levels. 
Various third-party tools offer management software to measure performance and other key indicators.
Other topics discuss simple approaches to systems monitoring and systems upgrading.
Hardware Failure
The complexity of computing systems has been increasing rapidly over the last decades. This has lead to 
hardware failures occurring more frequently and hence resulting in manufacturers providing solutions to 
reduce or even eliminate potential outages.
At the storage level, disk subsystems provide striping and mirroring capabilities with hot-swappable disk 
drives to provide redundancy. Redundant Array of Independent Disks (RAID) technologies are applied in 
layers to improve availability; RAID 10 and RAID 50 are examples of this approach.
Manufacturers offer servers with redundant components from power supplies, memory modules, 
processors, to hot-standby server systems. Fault tolerant solutions provide the best protection against 
outages by instantly switching to the redundant component in case of a failure. However, vendor 
proprietary hardware makes this approach cost intensive and it targets hardware failures only.
The requirements for redundancy depend on your business environment. The cost of high availability 
solutions can be tremendous and need to be justified based on the potential business impact of an outage.
High availability clustering is a cost effective solution that improves performance and enables off-load 
processing. In contrast to fault tolerant systems, high availability clusters target both hardware and 
software failures. Fail over switching is less efficient compared to the instant switching of fault tolerant 
technology, but is usually contained within a minute.
Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide B-35

=== PDF PAGE 236 ===
AppendixB:BestPractices
B-36 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
Software Failure
The highest point of contention is the database management system (DBMS), followed by the Application 
Server, and the Web server. Software management becomes ever more challenging with the intricate 
relationships of today's software components. Solutions establishing high availability of software 
components include systems monitoring, network load balancing and clustering. Network load balancing 
and clustering provide protection from software and hardware failures.
Each of these solutions has advantages and disadvantages.

=== PDF PAGE 237 ===
AppendixB:BestPractices
System Monitoring
Systems monitoring plays an important role in maintaining IT systems. It can be applied in various ways; 
detecting network congestion and system outages and determining system capacity and load are 
examples.
Systems monitoring should also be performed for operating systems, database management systems and  
Application Servers. Common means of exposing metrics are the Simple Network Monitoring Protocol 
(SNMP), Web-based Enterprise Management (WBEM), and Microsoft's Enterprise Instrumentation 
Framework (EIF).
Monitoring applications are available by many vendors and open-source groups. It is important to identify 
the protocols supported by your software and choose a monitoring application accordingly. Examples of 
monitoring applications include MOM 2005, Microsoft's Operations Manager, and IBM’s Tivoli.
A monitoring application is not an all-in-one solution. It requires active planning by you and your team. 
You should monitor the Application Server's performance to determine if the server is stressed and 
whether you need to add processors, servers, or memory. This list provides three ways to monitor 
performance:
• Use the Windows Performance Monitor application. Measure the server's state or activity using 
performance counters such as Transactions Per Second.
• Monitor CPU usage. Ensure that CPU usage is below 90%. Consider expanding CPU resources (16 
cores are recommended) or lowering the Application Server worker count  if the CPU is 
overloaded, but the Application Server memory is within limits. Taking this action enables the 
server to throttle throughput to an acceptable level.
• Monitor memory usage. Ensure that the private bytes memory counter for the Opcenter EX MDD 
or Opcenter EX CR processes stays under 3 GB. 
Note: The Application Server monitors its own memory and creates a new Application Server 
process and restarts the former process automatically if an exceptional condition 
occurs. It is a best practice to take steps to avoid this occurrence.
Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide B-37

=== PDF PAGE 238 ===
AppendixB:BestPractices
B-38 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
System Maintenance
System maintenance is categorized into hardware and software related issues. Hardware vendors provide 
detailed information on how to perform system maintenance, including upgrades to minimize downtime. 
In most cases, out of service scheduling is required.
Software updates to operating systems (OS) and database management systems may support rolling 
release upgrades to minimize downtime. For example, Oracle's DataGuard provides this feature. Obtain 
instructions and best practices  from your software vendor.
Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR require service outage for the application of Service Packs and 
releases. The overall downtime can be minimized when utilizing Network Load Balancing technology. The 
subject of NLB is covered in the next topic.

=== PDF PAGE 239 ===
AppendixB:BestPractices
Load Balancing
Load balancing evenly distributes transaction requests across multiple nodes in a server cluster, improving 
availability and throughput. Hardware and software load balancing solutions are supported by Opcenter 
EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR as explained in these topics.
Siemens recommends load balancing for the  Application Server. This enables you to apply Service Packs 
and  software updates to new releases gradually without taking down the entire system.
In a load balancing solution with four  Application Servers/Web Servers, the steps for applying a Service 
Pack could be: 
1. Take two servers out of service.
2. Apply the Service Pack to those two servers and leave those servers out of service until the object 
model has been updated.
3. Take the remaining active servers out of service.
4. Update the object model as necessary.
5. Bring the migrated servers online.
6. Apply the Service Pack to the remaining servers.
7. Bring the remaining servers online.
This approach reduces downtime and resource bandwidth required to apply a Service Pack. However, 
there will be a performance penalty due to the reduced number of servers in the cluster while performing 
upgrades to the first batch of servers.
A second, but more expensive, approach is the use of two load balancing clusters working 
interchangeably. Cluster A is the active cluster serving production and cluster B is used for development 
and validation purposes at the beginning of the migration process. 
A system upgrade is performed on cluster B first, while cluster A is still in production. After migrating 
cluster B, manufacturing is suspended for the time it takes to update the object model only. Cluster B takes 
over production services immediately after the update, swapping responsibility with cluster A. Cluster A 
can be migrated at a later time and be used for development and validation purposes. 
Applying   a new software update  could be approached similarly to the application of a  Service Pack.
Hardware Load Balancing
Hardware load balancing solutions are available from many network equipment vendors and require the 
installation of at least two equipment units for redundancy. The cost of a hardware load balancing solution 
is higher than the cost of a software load balancing solution. However, hardware load balancing 
eliminates the need for executing software processes on the server cluster and is superior in high-volume 
transaction environments. 
Siemens recommends hardware load balancing for environments requiring transaction volumes of 50 
transactions per second and above. 
Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide B-39

=== PDF PAGE 240 ===
AppendixB:BestPractices
B-40 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
Software Load Balancing
Network Load Balancing (NLB), a software load balancing solution, is a built-in feature of Microsoft server 
operating systems without incurring separate licensing costs. It improves performance by balancing 
service requests across multiple servers and protects from service outages by re-mapping requests from a 
failed system to an available system. The re-mapping takes place within ten seconds and is transparent to   
Portal. 
Note: Clusters are not application aware; therefore, if an Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR applic-
ation fails, the cluster will still send transactions to it.
A high availability solution needs to make all components of the application redundant. Typically the 
architecture is two-tiered, consisting of a Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR cluster and a high 
availability database cluster. (The database cluster is based on the recommendations of the database 
vendor and can, and frequently does, use a different clustering strategy than the Opcenter EX MDD or 
Opcenter EX CR cluster.)
The NLB service is fully distributed, eliminating a single-point of failure. NLB supports multiple network 
protocols and does not require specialized hardware. 
Remote management of NLB clusters is possible and supported by Microsoft Windows 2000 or later 
version.
NLB scales IP services across up to 32 nodes.   Portal uses a single IP address for cluster access for a variety 
of IP-based services like HTTP, VPN, among others. Siemens recommends cluster sizes of up to 8 nodes on 
a 100 BASE-T network.
Note: Teaming software, which allows two network cards to function as one card, should not be used 
with Network Load Balancing. It can cause a conflict that causes the Application Servers cached 
data to be out of sync.

=== PDF PAGE 241 ===
AppendixB:BestPractices
High Availability Clustering
The high availability clustering discussed in this topic focuses on the back end of a multi-tiered 
architecture, the database management system. It comprises both software and hardware components 
collaborating at the system level. Various vendors such as IBM, Oracle, and Microsoft offer clustering 
solutions.
High availability clusters are a set of shared resources with the goal of providing fail over and recovery 
from hardware and system failures. User intervention is not required in the event of a failure; fail over 
occurs automatically within a few seconds to a few minutes.
Storage systems play an important role in supporting high availability clusters. RAID technologies are 
commonly used, but also Serial Storage Architecture (SSA) solutions with fiber-optic connection are often 
utilized to provide redundancy. The disk subsystems are applied in single- and dual-configurations for 
additional redundancy. Server access to storage solutions can be non-concurrent or concurrent, depending 
on the storage solution and operating systems chosen. Non-concurrent access promotes one server as the 
owner of the storage system. Concurrent access provides storage access for each machine in the cluster, 
enabling all machines for processing.
In high availability architectures, the database management system is hosted on a cluster of servers. Each 
server  hosts the same databases and provides access to clients via a network connection. The architecture 
may require a direct communication link between the servers  in the cluster, depending on the solution 
vendor.
Close proximity is required between the servers; disaster recovery is not a feature of high availability 
clustering. Disaster recovery planning determines contingencies for potential disasters like floods and can 
be as simple as a data backup strategy.
Vendor solutions for high availability clustering of database servers may improve system performance, 
enable off-load processing, offer a scalable growth path, or simply provide fail over capabilities. 
Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide B-41

=== PDF PAGE 242 ===
AppendixB:BestPractices
B-42 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
Configurations according to the capabilities are referred to as idle standby, active standby, mutual 
takeover and balanced mutual takeover configurations. 
Oracle
Oracle's Real Application Cluster (RAC) solution is a software solution integrated into the database 
management system, including Oracle's Grid technology. 
Microsoft
Microsoft's offering is a software-based clustering solution included with Windows Advanced Server and 
Windows Data Center. Advanced Server supports two-node and Data Center support four-node cluster 
configurations.
Microsoft Cluster Service (MSCS) enables SQL Server , and it uses single, shared storage.  SQL Server High 
Availability option is required for use with Microsoft Cluster Services.

=== PDF PAGE 243 ===
AppendixB:BestPractices
Log Shipping
A log shipping solution is a database-level approach to maintain two identical databases, with one 
database being the production database and one database being the standby (backup) database. Log 
shipping does not require physical proximity of database servers, enabling disaster recovery.
Log shipping techniques are based on transfer and application of database log files from a primary 
database to a secondary database system (shown in the following diagram). The primary and secondary 
databases have physical and logical identity. Read access may be permitted to the secondary database, 
also referred to as standby database, but its status may lag the primary database.
Some DBMS vendors support zero transaction loss, preventing data loss in case of a system failure. Vendor 
specific techniques are discussed next.
Oracle
Oracle's DataGuard solution supports log shipping and replication. The DataGuard, when used in the REDO 
APPLY mode, leverages media recovery technology to transfer data from the production to the standby 
system. Media recovery technology enables read-only database availability.
Zero transaction loss is optional; the DataGuard is capable of writing to a local and remote log in a single 
commit cycle. A performance penalty needs to be accounted for when using the zero transaction loss 
feature.
Microsoft
SQL Server    supports transaction log shipping based on backup and restore functions. In the Microsoft 
scenario, a third server is recommended to monitor shipping from production to standby systems.
The SQL Server database is available for reporting purposes. A zero transaction loss feature is not available.
Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide B-43

=== PDF PAGE 244 ===
AppendixB:BestPractices
B-44 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
Replication
Replication is another solution similar to log shipping. However, in contrast to log shipping, it is based on a 
logical identity only and does not require physical identity of databases.
In replication mode, all database management systems supported by Siemens are available for transaction 
processing.
Refer to the current Release Matrix on the Support Center for information on supported database versions. 
Oracle
Oracle Database Advanced Replication and Oracle Streams are deprecated in Oracle 12c. Oracle 
GoldenGate should be used to replace Oracle Streams and all features of Advanced Replication including:
• Multimaster replication
• Updatable materialized views
• Hierarchical materialized views
• Deployment templates
Refer to the Oracle GoldenGate Integration white paper on Support Center for information on using 
GoldenGate.
SQL Server
SQL Server   has not introduced significant new features to SQL Server replication. SQL Server provides the 
following types of replication for use in distributed applications:
• Transactional replication
• Merge replication
• Snapshot replication
Transactional and Merge replication provide options for subsequent data changes to flow between the 
Publisher and the Subscriber incrementally over time. Transactional replication is widely used over Merge 
replication because of the usage of SQL Server transaction logs for tracking changes.
Third Party Replication Solutions
In addition to Oracle GoldenGate and the SQL Server native replication solutions, there are several third 
party replication solutions that could be used to provide logical replication of data. 

=== PDF PAGE 245 ===
AppendixB:BestPractices
FAQs: Troubleshooting Downtime and Errors
This topic explains how to resolve and recover from Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR downtime and 
errors after Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR has been installed and is running.
Changes Not Working in One Environment
I tested the changes and they worked in our test environment, but then they didn’t work in our production 
environment. Why would they work in one environment and not the other?
Answer
This normally occurs if your test environment and your production environment are in two different 
domains. You should always test in an environment that matches the production environment as close as 
possible, which means your test server should be in the active directory.
You should also make sure any leftover entries in the MSMQ Manager’s table are deleted.
XML Capture
Can I capture the XML being sent to the Application Server?
Answer
Yes, there are  ways to capture this information:
• Turn on the flag (set it to True) for LogXMLDocument in the  WCF Services web.config file. It will 
generate a document under c:\temp directory. This will log xml input from the Portal.  
• Use the asXML method on the document just before the submit. This will return the string that is 
sent to the server. This requires a code change.
Memory Leaks
We think we are having an access violation or exception due to memory leaks. However, there are also 
.report files in our Server directory. How can we determine the source of the problem?
Answer
You should open a ticket with Product Support and work with them to debug and resolve this error. 
Txn Tracing in Multi-Server Environment
Why is my transaction tracing not working in a multi-server environment?
We have two Application Servers, and sometimes stop one. Why are some transactions such as  compound 
transactions slow after we stop one?
Answer
Both questions above are problems that can result if you have invalid entries in the MSMQManagers table.
If you have an App Server stop or die unexpectedly, then its entry is left in the MSMQManagers table. This 
bad entry in the MSMQManagers table is what causes the problem. When Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter 
Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide B-45

=== PDF PAGE 246 ===
AppendixB:BestPractices
B-46 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
EX CR tries to send the message to the stopped server that an update has occurred (to keep the caches in 
sync), it cannot make the connection because the server is not available. 
Unfortunately, the system has to wait for the Microsoft library call to time out before control is returned to 
Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR. Hence, the slow response time on transactions. 
Cleaning up (removing the unused entry) the MSMQManagers table should resolve these issues. You 
should only delete the bad entry. Its entry will be re-added when the server is restarted. 
Note: You can only truncate the table if you stop all the Application Servers first. Then the entries will 
be re-added when you restart your Application Servers.
System Hangs
What do I do if my system hangs?
How to Troubleshoot a System Hang
Follow these steps to troubleshoot a system hang:
1. Try to connect to the database via a database utility like Toad or SQL Developer (For Oracle) or 
SQL Server Management Studio (for SQL Server).
2. Verify you can run a simple query like Select * from Factory.
3.  Use DebugView to check for any messages indicating there are problems.
4. Check the Windows event logs (via Event Viewer) for any error around the time of the hang. 
Please review all areas (Application, Security, System and  Camstar event log) for errors.
5. Check for high memory usage (out of virtual memory).
6. Check the ...\Camstar\InSite Server directory for any .report files indicating that access violations 
have occurred.
7. If any of these files are found, then please upload the corresponding  dump files (files with the 
.dmp file extension) to  your Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR FTP upload site.
8. Gather a memory dump of the W3WP.exe process which hosts the OpCenter EX Core application 
pool:
a. Start Windows Task Manager.
b. Click on the Details tab. (You may need to click the More details button to see the tabs.)
c. Right-click any column (such as Name) and click on Select columns. Make sure the PID (Pro-
cess Identifier) and Command line are checked, then click OK.
d. Click the Image path name column header to sort by that column and scroll down to find all 
instances of the w3wp processes.
There may be more than one. Scroll to the right, and find the instance of w3wp in which the 
command line references the CamstarAppServer application pool, such as:
c:\windows\system32\inetsrv\w3wp.exe -ap "CamstarAppServer" . . .
e. Right-click on this process and select Create dump file. The Dumping process dialog box 
appears. The location of the memory dump file is listed in the dialog box when the process 
completes.
f. Click OK and then upload the file to Product Support.

=== PDF PAGE 247 ===
AppendixB:BestPractices
9. Start an elevated command prompt, and execute this command:
netstat -anob > c:\netstat.txt
10. Contact Product Support for additional help and to verify that you have gathered all the needed 
information.
Memory Cache Refreshed
How often is the memory cache refreshed?
Answer
Cache is refreshed when you apply metadata changes using Management Studio. The cache is also 
refreshed on a scheduled interval. The default interval is 60 minutes, but this procedure allows you to 
change the interval.
How to Change the Scheduled Interval
Follow these steps to change the scheduled interval: 
1. Click Start >  Opcenter Execution Core > Management Studio. The Management Studio 
window appears.
2. Expand the Configuration folder in the Sites tree and select the Configuration node. The site 
configuration information appears in the right pane.
3. Change the value in the Flush the data cache every __ Minutes field (General Tab) to the 
preferred interval.
4. Click Save. Management Studio displays a success message.
Caching Model Memory Issues
Now that we have a lot of entries in our tables, we are experiencing memory issues that we think are 
based on the caching model. What can we do?
Answer
You need to analyze your model and make the appropriate changes in Designer. Product Support can help 
you with your analysis.
I/O Contention
Is there anything we can do to relieve I/O contention or make our queries more efficient?
Answer
If you have inefficient queries or indexes, contact Product Support for a tuning engagement. Performance 
tuning can help you meet your performance goals and metrics.
How to Stop the Application Server
Follow these steps to stop the Application Server:
1. Click Start > Opcenter Execution MDD > Management Studio. The Management Studio 
window appears.
Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide B-47

=== PDF PAGE 248 ===
AppendixB:BestPractices
B-48 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
Or
Click Start >  Opcenter Execution Core > Management Studio. The Management Studio win-
dow appears.
2. Expand the Administration folder and select the Services node. The Services information 
appears in the right pane.
3. Select a server from the Services for <server> field. The Services grid refreshes and displays a 
list of services and their current status.
4. Right-click Camstar App Server and select Stop Service. The Application Server's status changes 
to Stopped.
How to Update the Database with the New Password
Refer to  "How to Update the Transaction Database" for  information.
How to Restart the  Application Server
Follow these steps to restart the Application Server:

window appears.

dow appears.
2. Expand the Administration folder and select the Services node. The Services information 
appears in the right pane.
3. Select a server from the Services for <server> field. The Services grid refreshes and displays a 
list of services and their current status.
4. Right-click Camstar App Server and select Start Service. The Application Server''s status changes 
to Running.

Upgrading to Release 2510
This topic identifies some key considerations for  upgrading from Version 8 or earlier of Opcenter Execution 
Core or Opcenter Execution Medical Device and Diagnostics to Release 2510. Refer to the  Opcenter 
Execution Core Installation Guide or the Opcenter Execution Medical Device and Diagnostics Installation 
Guidefor  complete information.
Upgrading MDB Files
You must run the Replace Workspace Data utility to convert your metadata, and run the Industry Solutions 
Configuration Tool to update any industry or CIO workspaces if you are upgrading. Files needed for the 
industry solution features are provided as part of the installation. You must run the Industry Solutions 
Configuration Tool to configure them.
Applying Custom Stylesheets
Siemens provides the ability for you to apply custom stylesheets to the application. The user.css file is an 
empty placeholder file for your custom stylesheets. It is located here by default:
Camstar\CamstarPortal\Themes\User
Software updates do not affect the user.css file. When you apply a  software update, your customizations 
in the user.css file will not be overwritten.

Introduction
The Message Center is an extension of the Concierge, therefore the Message Center page permission is 
required for each user who performs Portal transactions. This section identifies the transactions and the 
security permissions that are associated with each  page. It also identifies Portal permissions associated 
with each page and pageflow.

• System, Security Administration, Portal Configuration, General, and Inquiry Permissions

Appendix D: Copying a Database 
from One Environment to Another
Introduction
These procedures enable you to copy one Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR environment to another 
environment; for example, when you want to copy the production environment to the development 
environment for test purposes.
Review these procedures carefully before performing them. Siemens is not liable for issues that occur due 
to failure to follow these instructions.  Contact Product Support if you have any questions about these 
procedures.
In This Appendix
This appendix contains this topic:
• Copying the Transaction (OLTP) Database
• Copying the DataStore  Database

=== PDF PAGE 276 ===
AppendixD:CopyingaDatabasefromOneEnvironmenttoAnother
D-2 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
Copying the Transaction (OLTP) Database
This procedure enables you to create a copy of your OLTP database.
How to Copy the OLTP Database
Follow these steps to copy the OLTP database:
1. Stop all product-related application pools and services on all Application Server hosts: the 
Application Server, the  Deployment Server, the  Notification Server, and the  Security Server. 
Note: Refer to the Opcenter Execution Medical Device and Diagnostics Installation Guide or 
the Opcenter Execution Core Installation Guide for information on the best order to 
stop and start application pools and services.
2. Create a copy of the source OLTP database: 
• SQL Server:  Create a backup.
• Oracle: Export the schema.
Important:Do not include indexes, stored procedures or sequences. 
3. Apply this copy to the target database: 
Important:Siemens assumes that database users were created with necessary Opcenter EX 
MDD or Opcenter EX CR specific permissions.
• SQL Server:  Restore the database and  fix the db user.
• Oracle:  Import the schema.
Confirm that the MSMQManagers and SecurityServers tables are empty.  If not, then delete the 
entries. (Truncate the tables; do not drop the tables.)
4. Run the stored procedure, csiResetTxnID. 
5. Confirm all settings in Management Studio to verify that no references are made to the 
production system resources.
6. Go to Management Studio, expand the appropriate Site, and then expand the Databases folder.
7. Right-click Transaction Database, and then click Update Database. The Update Database dialog 
box appears. 
8. Leave the default check box selections on the Update Database dialog box and click OK. 
9. Start the Application Server application pool.

=== PDF PAGE 277 ===
AppendixD:CopyingaDatabasefromOneEnvironmenttoAnother
Copying the DataStore  Database
Siemens does not recommend copying DataStore databases from one environment to another due to 
environment-specific references internal to a database. If you need a DataStore environment for 
development or testing purposes, rebuild the DataStore using the procedure for creating a new database 
from an existing OLTP. Refer to the  DataStore installation checklists in the Opcenter Execution Medical 
Device and Diagnostics DataStore Reference Guide or the Opcenter ExecutionCore DataStore Reference 
Guide for information.
Important:Contact Product Support for assistance if you need an exact copy of your existing 
database.

Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide E-1
Appendix E: Application Server 
Transaction Retry
Introduction
The Application Server Retry transaction feature allows the Application Server to reprocess a failed 
transaction. The feature requires requests to include an element that specifies a given transaction can be 
reprocessed.
In This Appendix
This appendix contains this topic:
• Application Server Transaction Retry Feature

=== PDF PAGE 280 ===
AppendixE:ApplicationServerTransactionRetry
E-2 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
Application Server Transaction Retry Feature
The Application Server Retry transaction feature allows the Application Server to reprocess a failed 
transaction. The feature requires requests to include an element that specifies a given transaction can be 
reprocessed.
The element includes this information:
• An explicit retry count, and
• Whether a retry is explicitly allowed, denied, or default behavior.
The Application Server does not reprocess failed transactions by default.
Siemens provides support for using a query string parameter as the element. In addition to the properties 
that can be specified in the parameter, there will be some configuration options to allow specifying some 
defaults.
The retry feature element structure is provided as part of a transaction context element structure. The 
structure is on the query string parameter. The transaction context and any properties in it, including the 
retry ones, are all optional. The transaction context will be extensible for use in future feature 
implementations.
The name of the query string parameter is transactionContext. 
The name of the HTTP request header is x-appserver-transactioncontext. 
The name of the InSite XML node is __transactionContext. 
The name of the appSettings key in the web.config is AppServerTransactionDefaults.
The name of the element is retry. The property names and possible values for the retry feature element are 
as follows:
• enabled – Enable or disable retry. You can set the enabled property only at the server level in the 
AppServerTransactionDefaults app setting.
Possible values:
• true – enables the feature. Setting the enabled property to true does not mean retry 
behavior will occur—only that retry is enabled and can be requested either at a default level 
and/or a request level. This property must be present and set to true in the 
AppserverTransactionDefaults app setting.
• false – disables the feature
• null – disables the feature. This is the default value.
The default value of null is the same as false in this implementation.
• allow - Allow or deny the retry.
Possible values:
• true – explicitly allows the retry
• false – explicitly denies the retry
• null – specifies to use the default setting specified in the App Server transaction processing 
settings. This is the default value.

=== PDF PAGE 281 ===
AppendixE:ApplicationServerTransactionRetry
Setting the allow value to null is the same as setting it to false or not providing a value at all since 
the default without configuration is to not retry transactions.
• max – Maximum number of times to retry a failed transaction.
Possible values:
• -1 – explicitly means unlimited
• [0..maxInt] – explicitly means to use the default setting specified in the App Server 
transaction processing settings. This is the default setting.
Setting the max value to 0 is the same as no retry or not providing a value at all since the default 
without any configuration is 0.
• delayMs – Minimum number of milliseconds to wait between retries.
Possible values:
• [0..maxInt] – The default setting is 0.
Setting the delayMs property to 0 means the application does not wait before a retry attempt 
regardless of the delayFlags setting. 
Setting the delayMs property to a non-zero value when the delayFlags property is set to fixed 
means the application waits the specified number of milliseconds to wait between successive 
retries. 
Setting the delayMs property to a non-zero value when the delayFlags property is set to exp 
means the application waits the specified number of milliseconds to wait for the first retry and 
then exponentially increases the wait time between successive retries.
• delayMsMax – Maximum number of milliseconds to wait between retries. The value must be 0 or 
greater than or equal to the delayMs value.
Possible values:
• 0 – Specifies no maximum wait time. This is the default setting.
•  [delayMs..maxInt] – Explicitly states the maximum number of milliseconds to wait between 
retries.
Note: This setting only applies when the delayFlags value is set to exp. Additionally, this 
property does not specify the total amount of time for a given transaction. The total 
amount of time is controlled by the combination of retry max and the cumulative 
delay of each retry.
• delayFlags – Specifies whether the application waits a fixed amount of time between retries or 
exponentially increases the delay between successive retries.
Possible values:
• exp – Specifies to use an exponential delay. This setting doubles the wait time between 
each attempt. This is the default setting. 
• fixed – Specifies to use the same wait time between attempts.
• conditions – Include or exclude arrays.
Possible values:
• null. This is the default setting.
Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide E-3

=== PDF PAGE 282 ===
AppendixE:ApplicationServerTransactionRetry
E-4 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
• an include array, an exlude array, or both.
If the conditions value is null or a value is not supplied, the default setting specified in the App 
Server transaction processing settings is used. The default behavior without any configuration is 
that all transaction failure conditions are included except login or security (RBAC) failures.
• include – Failure conditions to include.
Possible values:
• null. This is the default setting.
• an array of condition objects.
If the include value is null, no failure conditions are included.
• exclude – Failure conditions to exclude.
Possible values:
• null. This is the default setting.
• an array of condition objects.
If the exclude value is null, then no failure conditions are excluded from any included conditions.
• condition – may include an exception object in this implementation
Possible values:
• null. This is the default setting.
• an exception object.
If the condition value is null, the condition is ignored.
• exception – exception codes to include or exclude.
Possible values:
• null. This is the default setting.
• code. The code member can be either a number or an array of numbers. 
Server Configurations
Following is the minimum server configuration needed to use the retry feature:
{"retry":{"enabled":true}}
Following is the minimum server configuration needed to have the retry feature automatically retry 
transactions without requiring a request level setting:
{"retry":{"enabled":true,"allow":true,"max":1}}
A recommended exception code to use for either a default configuration or a per-request configuration is 
13500638, which is named InvalidChangeCount. Exceptions of this type have the error text: "The object 
#ErrorMsg.CDOTypeName with instance id #ErrorMsg.CDOID has been modified by someone else.  Please 
redo your changes."
This is the effective retry defaults with no configuration set in the web.config.
{
"retry": {

"allow": false,

Using the IIS Manager to Set the Default Configuration
Follow these steps to set the default retry element in IIS:
1. Open the Server Manager. 
2. Select Internet Information Services (IIS) Manager from the Tools menu. 
3. Expand the server in the Connections pane.
4. Expand Default Web Site and select CamstarAppServer.
5. Open the Configuration Editor.
6. Select the appSettings section.
7. Click into the collection element and then click the ellipsis to open the appSettings Collection 
Editor.
8. Click Add on the right.
9. Enter AppServerTransactionDefaults in the key textbox in the Properties pane.
10. Enter the JSON configuration string in the value textbox: 
{ "retry": { "enabled": true, "allow": true, "max": 1, "conditions": { "include": [ { "exception": { 
"code": 13500638 } } ] } } }
11. Click the Windows Close button on the Collection Editor.
12. Click Apply on the right to save the settings.

Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide F-1
Appendix F: Smart Scanning
 
Introduction
The Smart Scanning feature is designed for operators scanning barcodes during shop floor operations. 
Smart Scanning enables the application to automatically populate page fields when the operator scans 
barcodes, reducing operator data entry and the chance for typing errors. Two types of Smart Scanning can 
be implemented—default and Rule-Based:
• Default Smart Scanning uses configured prefixes (smart tags) to identify scannable data 
elements.
• Rule-Based Smart Scanning uses configured patterns to identify scannable data elements and 
thus does not require the barcode to contain prefixes. Rule-Based Smart Scanning includes the 
option to automate page actions such as clicking a Save button.
Important:You can implement default Smart Scanning or Rule-Based Smart Scanning, but not both.
In This Appendix
This appendix contains these topics:
• Understanding Smart Scanning
• Enabling the Smart Scanning Feature
• Defining Prefixes or Smart Tags
• Defining Smart Scan Rules (Rule-Based Smart Scanning Only)
• Enabling Scanning on Portal Pages
• Default Smart Scanning Scenarios

=== PDF PAGE 286 ===
AppendixF:SmartScanning
F-2 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
Understanding Smart Scanning
Smart scanning is available for Portal Shop Floor and Modeling pages. Smart Scanning allows the 
application to populate one or more fields when you scan a barcode. For example, scanning a barcode on 
a container could populate not only the Container field, but also other fields displaying information about 
that container. 
A smart barcode may include the following elements:
• A preamble—a single character indicating whether the barcode is a smart barcode. When the 
system detects a preamble, it identifies the barcode as smart and strips the preamble before 
reading the associated content.
• A prefix or "smart-tag" attached to a scan string indicating the type of data the string contains. 
This prefix includes the entity or field name to be populated with the corresponding barcode 
data. Prefix examples include: #Carrier#, #Container#, and #Batch#. The prefix is used in default 
Smart Scanning.
Note: Default Smart Scanning looks for the prefixes or smart tags within a smart barcode. Rule-Based 
Smart Scanning looks for patterns of data that match patterns configured on a Smart Scan 
Rule.
How Smart Scanning Works
For Smart Scanning to be available for a page, the Smart Scanning feature must be enabled and at least 
one field must have a value set in its SmartScanTypeName property.  For Rule-Based Smart Scanning, the 
application also checks whether a Smart Scan Rule is configured on the Factory of the Resource of the 
logged-on user’s line assignment and whether the rule is active.
If Smart Scanning is available for the page, the application looks for the preamble indicating that the 
barcode is smart. Automatic field population varies between default and Rule-Based Smart Scanning.
For default Smart Scanning, the application looks for prefixes or smart tags within the barcode.
For Rule-Based Smart Scanning, the application looks for patterns of  barcode data that match patterns 
defined in a Smart Scan Rule. When a pattern match is found, a check is done to see whether the Smart 
Scan Rule pattern is active.
Default Smart Scanning     Barcode Example
This image shows a sample barcode used in default Smart Scanning.
In this example:
• ! is the preamble
• #Container# is the prefix or smart tag
• SCA.0001 is the container name value to be placed into the Container field on the page.

=== PDF PAGE 287 ===
AppendixF:SmartScanning
When a shop floor operator scans this barcode, the application strips the ! from the scan string, locates the 
Container field, and populates the field with SCA.0001.
Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide F-3

=== PDF PAGE 288 ===
AppendixF:SmartScanning
F-4 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
Enabling the Smart Scanning Feature
By default, the smart scanning feature (barcode scanning) is disabled. You enable the feature by 
modifying a setting in Portal Studio, specifically in the SmartScanningSettings section. 
Remember that although you are enabling the smart scanning feature in Portal Studio, you must still 
enable and disable smart scanning on individual pages and fields.
Smart Scanning Settings in Portal Studio
Use Portal Studio to enable and configure the smart scanning feature. The Smart Scanning settings are 
configured in Portal Settings within Portal Studio. 
This image shows an example of smart scanning settings in Portal Studio.
At this level, you can do the following:
• Enable or disable the feature.
• Identify the character (preamble) that indicates a barcode is Smart.
• Click the More button to view defined scanner templates (smart tags).
• Identify the control character (terminator) that the scanning device will append to the barcode.
Important:Siemens recommends that you use the Tab key as the default terminator. If you 
choose to create a custom terminator, it is recommended that use one of the 
special characters as described on the w3schools website: 
https://www.w3schools.com/js/js_strings.asp

=== PDF PAGE 289 ===
AppendixF:SmartScanning
Smart Scanning Settings in the Settings.xml File
Although you typically enable and customize the scanning feature using Portal Studio , you can update the 
Settings.xml file manually. Changes made to the smart scanning settings in Portal Studio are visible in the 
Settings.xml file, and changes made manually in the Settings.xml file appear in Portal Studio. This is the 
default location for the Settings.xml file: C:\Program Files (x86)\Camstar\Camstar Portal\User
The top level XML node is used to enable or disable the Smart Scanning feature. The next level node 
contains individual scanner template nodes which equate to smart tags.
This image shows an example of default settings in the SmartScanningSettings section of the xml file.
Important:Siemens recommends that you use the Tab key as the default terminator in the 
Settings.xml file.
How to Enable the Smart Scanning Feature in the Portal
Follow these steps to enable the smart scanning feature in the Portal:
1. Access Portal Studio.
2. Click the Portal Settings button. The Portal Settings window appears.
3. Click SmartScanningSettings. Portal Studio loads the settings in the right pane.
4. Locate the Enabled setting.
5. Change the Enabled setting to True.
6. Click OK to close the Portal Settings window.
Note:  Refer to “How to Enable Default Smart Scanning for a Field" for information on 
enabling smart scanning for a specific Portal field or entity.
Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide F-5

=== PDF PAGE 290 ===
AppendixF:SmartScanning
F-6 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
Defining Prefixes or Smart Tags
The application allows you to define prefixes or smart tags in Portal Studio. These prefixes are embedded 
within the barcodes used by default Smart Scanning.  A prefix or smart tag contains both a prefix "special" 
character followed by a field entity name. 
Default Prefixes or Smart Tags
This table lists field entities and their  prefixes or smart tags provided with the application by default: 
Field Entity Prefix or Smart Tag
Container #Container#
Carrier #Carrier#
Batch #Batch#
LotCode #LotCode#
MfgOrder #MfgOrder#
Operator #Operator#
Product #Product#
Quantity #Quantity#
Identifier #Identifier#
Generally, all text boxes and named objects on the shop floor pages can be scanned. (Grids in the 
application are an exception and scanning is possible only if externally bound controls are used.) You will 
need to define a prefix or smart tag for the additional entity and enable smart scanning for that entity. You 
can define prefixes or smart tags in Portal Studio where they are referred to as scanner templates.
This image shows a prefix or smart tag definition in Portal Studio.
Note: You can also define prefixes or smart tags in the SmartScanningSettings section of the 
Settings.xml file also. Refer to “Smart Scanning Settings in the Settings.xml File” for  
information.

=== PDF PAGE 291 ===
AppendixF:SmartScanning
Defining Prefixes or Smart Tags to Distinguish Same Type Fields
When you have multiple fields of the same type on a page, you can define prefixes or smart tags to 
distinguish between the fields based on your business need. 
Example
The Disassociate page has two fields for container entry: the Container field in the header and the Enter 
Container to Select field. You can use the default Container smart tag for the Container field in the header 
and define a custom tag for the Enter Container to Select field to ensure the application populates the 
correct fields.
How to Define a Prefix or Smart Tag
Follow these steps to define a prefix or smart tag (scanner template):
1. Open Portal Studio.
2. Click the Portal Settings button. The Portal Settings window appears.
3. Click SmartScanningSettings. Portal Studio loads the settings in the right pane.
4. Click the More button for ScannerTemplates (Collection). Portal Studio displays the 
ScannerTemplates.
5. Click Add. Portal Studio adds a scanner template to the list in the left pane and blank fields in 
the right pane.
6. Enter the  Name of the template.
7. Enter the  Prefix or Smart Tag.
Note: The Prefix will consist of the pound symbol before and after the field entity name.
8. Click OK. Portal Studio saves the scanner template and closes the ScannerTemplates window.
9. Click OK to close the Portal Settings window.
Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide F-7

=== PDF PAGE 292 ===
AppendixF:SmartScanning
F-8 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
Defining Smart Scan Rules (Rule-Based Smart Scanning 
Only)
Rule-Based Smart Scanning does not rely upon barcodes containing prefixes or smart tags. It relies upon 
barcode data conforming to patterns defined in a Smart Scan Rule. The application knows to process a 
segment of barcode if the segment scanned conforms to a configured pattern. Processing in Rule-Based 
Smart Scanning can include automatic Portal page field population as well as automated on-click event 
processing, such as what would occur when the operator clicks the Save button.
This image shows an example of a Smart Scan Rule with two patterns.
How to Define a Smart Scan Rule with a Pattern
Follow these steps to define a Smart Scan Rule:
1. Open the Smart Scan Rule page in Modeling. The Smart Scan Rule page appears within the 
Modeling page.
2. Click New. Blank fields appear for you to define a new instance.
3. Enter a preamble value in the Preamble field.
4. Enter the terminator character in the Terminator field.
5. Enter the characters identifying the pattern in the Pattern column of the Patterns grid.
6. Enter the Smart Scan Type to identify the Portal page field to be populated.
7. Click Save. The application saves the modeling object and displays a success message.
Note: Refer to "Defining Smart Scan Rules" in the Opcenter Execution Medical Device and Diagnostics 
Modeling User Guide or the Opcenter Execution Core Modeling User Guide.

=== PDF PAGE 293 ===
AppendixF:SmartScanning
Enabling Scanning on Portal Pages
 Both default and Rule-Based Smart Scanning require the following conditions to be met:
• Smart Scanning enabled in Portal Studio.
• At least one field in the page with the SmartScanTypeName property set.
Important:The SmartScanTypeName property contains a drop-down list for text fields. Otherwise, 
you must manually enter the value.
How to Enable Default Smart Scanning for a Field
Follow these steps to enable default Smart Scanning for any Portal page field:
1. Ensure you have enabled the Smart Scanning feature.
2. Ensure you have defined a prefix or smart tag for the field.
3. Access Portal Studio.
4. Double-click the page name in the Components pane. The page layout appears.
5. Select the field for which you want to enable smart scanning. Portal Studio updates the 
Properties pane with settings specific to the selected field.
6. Locate the SmartScanTypeName property.
7. Select or enter the prefix or smart tag. The SmartScanTypeName value must match the prefix 
exactly.
8. Save your changes.
How to Enable Rule-Based Smart Scanning for a Field
Follow these steps to enable Rule-Based Smart Scanning for a Portal page field:
1. Ensure you have enabled the Smart Scanning feature.
2. Ensure you have defined a Smart Scan Rule with an appropriate pattern and Smart Scan Type for 
the field.
3. Access Portal Studio.
4. Double-click the page name in the Components pane. The page layout appears.
5. Select the field for which you want to enable smart scanning. Portal Studio updates the 
Properties pane with settings specific to the selected field.
6. Locate the SmartScanTypeName property.
7. Select the Smart Scan Type in the SmartScanType property.
8. Save your changes.
Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide F-9

=== PDF PAGE 294 ===
AppendixF:SmartScanning
F-10 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
Default Smart Scanning Scenarios
The field in which the application populates the entity name depends on the information contained in the 
barcode and whether the shop floor page is smart scan-enabled. These are possible scenarios:
• Smart Scan-Enabled page and Smart Barcode
• Smart Scan-Enabled page and Non Smart Barcode
• Smart Scan-Disabled page
This table describes what the Portal does in each scenario.
If the page 
is Smart 
Scan . . .
And the 
barcode is 
. . .
Then the application does the following:
Enabled Smart Locates the target field, strips the smart tag, and populates the field 
with the entity name.
Example:
Smart barcode: !#Container#SCA.0001
Result: SCA.0001
If the application cannot find the target field, it displays an error 
message indicating that the field does not exist. For example, if you 
scan a container on a page for which there is no Container field, the 
application displays an error message.
Enabled Not Smart Populates the field the cursor is on with the barcode contents.
Example:
Not smart barcode: SCA.0001
Result: SCA.0001
Disabled N/A Populates the field the cursor is on with the barcode contents, 
regardless of whether the barcode is smart.
Example 1:
Smart barcode: !#Container#SCA.0001
Result: !#Container#SCA.0001
Example 2:
Not smart barcode: SCA.0001
Result: SCA.0001
Upon populating the scanned field and its dependent fields, the application moves the cursor to the next 
field.
The scenarios described above assume that the barcode has a terminator (defined in the 
SmartScanningSettings section of the Portal configuration file). The terminator defined in the 
configuration file matches the actual terminator sent by the scanning device. If the barcode is not sent 
with a terminator, the application does not move the cursor to the next field and the barcode type (Smart 
or Not Smart) determines whether the application populates the field.
If the barcode is Not Smart, the application populates the field the cursor is on with the entity name and 
the cursor sits at the end of the last character. If the barcode is Smart, the application populates the field 
when the focus is removed from the target field. Regardless of barcode type, the focus may be removed 

=== PDF PAGE 295 ===
AppendixF:SmartScanning
automatically by the scanning device if a terminator has been configured for the device or manually by 
tabbing off of the target field.
Note: The terminator coming from the scanning device must match the terminator value defined in 
the  configuration file.   Refer to “Smart Scanning Settings in Portal Studio” for information on 
defining terminators for the Portal. For information about configuring a terminator for a scan-
ning device, refer to the documentation supplied with the device.

Appendix G: Setting Up the SMTP 
Server
Introduction
You must add a Simple Mail Transfer Protocol (SMTP) server after installing Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter 
EX CR.
In This Appendix
This appendix contains this topic:
• Setting Up an SMTP Server On Windows Server 2019
 

=== PDF PAGE 298 ===
AppendixG:SettingUptheSMTPServer
G-2 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
Setting Up an SMTP Server On Windows Server 2019
You must set up an SMTP server after installing Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR.
When Setting Up an SMTP Server on Windows Server 2019
You use the Add Roles and Features Wizard to add the SMTP server. You then use Internet Information 
Services (IIS) Manager to configure the service for the default web site. Ensure the Notification and SMTP 
Servers are started after you successfully configure the service.
How to Set Up an SMTP Server on Windows Server 2019
Follow these steps to set up an SMTP server on Windows Server 2019:
1. Click Start > Server Manager. The Server Manager window appears.
2. Select Add Roles and Features from the Manage menu. The Add Roles and Features Wizard 
appears and displays the Before you begin page.
3. Click Next. The Select Installation type page appears.
4. Ensure Role-based or feature-based installation is selected and click Next. The Select 
destination server page appears.
5. Ensure Select a server from the server pool is selected and select the server you want to set up 
an SMTP server on from the list of available servers. Then click Next. The Select server roles page 
appears.
6. Click Next. The Select features page appears.
7. Select SMTP Server. The Add Roles and Features Wizard pop-up appears.
8. Click Add Features. The application adds the features and closes the Add Roles and Features 
Wizard pop-up.
9. Click Next. The Confirmation installation selections page appears.
10. Click Install. The Installation progress page appears and begins the installation process.
11. Click Close when you see the success message "Installation succeeded on ...". The application 
adds the SMTP server and closes the Add Roles and Features Wizard.
How to Configure the Service for the Default Web Site
Follow these steps to configure the service for the default web site:
1. Click Start > Server Manager. The Server Manager window appears.
2. Select Internet Information Services (IIS) Manager from the Tools menu. The Internet 
Information Services (IIS) Manager window appears.
3. Expand the server in the Connections pane.

=== PDF PAGE 299 ===
AppendixG:SettingUptheSMTPServer
4. Expand the Sites folder and select Default Web Site. The Default Web Site Home window 
appears.
5. Double-click SMTP E-mail. The SMTP E-mail window appears.
6. Select Store e-mail in pickup directory and enter the path in the field.
7. Click  Apply in the Actions pane. A message indicating the changes were saved successfully 
appears in the Alerts pane.
How to Ensure the Notification and SMTP Servers are Started
Follow these steps to ensure the Notification  and SMTP servers are started:
1. Click Start > Windows Administrative Tools > Services. The Services window appears.
2. Ensure  the Camstar Notification Server and the Simple Mail Transfer Protocol (SMTP) are running.
Release2510+Rev.1 SystemAdministrationGuide G-3

Appendix H: ASCII Character Codes
Introduction
The Portal  employs American Standard Code for Information Interchange (ASCII) for character encoding. 
This section contains a full table of ALT-ASCII values for use in the  application as well as guidelines for 
using ALT-ASCII codes.
In This Appendix
This appendix contains this topic:
• ALT-ASCII Codes

=== PDF PAGE 302 ===
AppendixH:ASCIICharacterCodes
H-2 SystemAdministrationGuide Release2510+Rev.1
ALT-ASCII Codes
This table identifies ALT-ASCII codes, the associated character value and whether they are supported in the 
Portal.
The following are guidelines to be aware of when using ALT-ASCII codes:
• The space character (ALT-32) cannot be used as the leading or trailing character in objects, such 
as container names, NDO names, RO names, revisions, or enumerations.
• Some combinations of characters cause errors, such as the CDATA block of “]]”.
• Some combinations of characters that mirror common programming language conventions may 
cause errors. For example, the combination of "< string >" can cause an error and is not supported 
in the Portal.
• The combination of the characters "##" cannot be used within a container name.
• Double-byte characters are supported in the Portal.
• All of the character support information is based on having an English operating system and an 
English database.
